> AGENT

Now I see the full picture. Here's the root cause:

## Root Cause

The upload is a **two-step process**:

1. **`POST /protocols/upload`** — Returns a signed/local upload URL and creates the DB record + fires the `ProtocolUploaded` event
2. **The client must then `PUT` the actual PDF bytes** to the returned upload URL (`http://localhost:8000/local-upload/{blob_path}`)

The problem is a **race condition**: The `ProtocolUploaded` event is published via the outbox **before the client has had a chance to PUT the actual file**. The outbox processor picks it up and the protocol processor tries to read the file, but it doesn't exist yet because the client hasn't uploaded it.

The directory `cb6e58bd-27d4-45b7-912f-d5c6dc1f6b2c/` was created by `_local_generate_upload_url` (line 160 of gcs.py), but the file inside it is empty because the PUT never completed (or never happened) before the processor tried to read it.

## Two possible scenarios:

1. **The frontend isn't calling the PUT** to the upload URL after getting the signed URL back
2. **The event fires too early** — the outbox publishes `ProtocolUploaded` immediately on commit, before the client finishes the actual file upload

Given this is local dev mode, **scenario 2 is most likely** — the event is committed to the outbox at the same time as the protocol record, and the outbox processor (running every 1s) picks it up and triggers processing before the browser has time to PUT the file.

## Fix Options

The cleanest fix: **Don't fire the event from the upload endpoint.** Instead, add a confirmation step or fire the event only after the file is actually received. Would you like me to implement a fix where:

- The `local-upload` PUT endpoint confirms the file was received, and **that's** when the `ProtocolUploaded` event gets published (or the protocol status transitions to trigger processing)?

Or alternatively, a simpler approach: have the protocol processor **retry with a short delay** when the file isn't found yet?

> DEVELOPER

     1→import * as Dialog from '@radix-ui/react-dialog';
     2→import { Loader2, Upload, X } from 'lucide-react';
     3→import { type DragEvent, useCallback, useRef, useState } from 'react';
     4→
     5→import { useUploadProtocol } from '../hooks/useProtocols';
     6→import { cn } from '../lib/utils';
     7→import { Button } from './ui/Button';
     8→
     9→interface ProtocolUploadDialogProps {
    10→    open: boolean;
    11→    onOpenChange: (open: boolean) => void;
    12→}
    13→
    14→const MAX_FILE_SIZE = 50 * 1024 * 1024; // 50MB
    15→
    16→export function ProtocolUploadDialog({ open, onOpenChange }: ProtocolUploadDialogProps) {
    17→    const [selectedFile, setSelectedFile] = useState<File | null>(null);
    18→    const [error, setError] = useState<string | null>(null);
    19→    const [dragOver, setDragOver] = useState(false);
    20→    const fileInputRef = useRef<HTMLInputElement>(null);
    21→
    22→    const uploadMutation = useUploadProtocol();
    23→
    24→    const validateFile = useCallback((file: File): string | null => {
    25→        if (file.type !== 'application/pdf') {
    26→            return 'Only PDF files are accepted';
    27→        }
    28→        if (file.size > MAX_FILE_SIZE) {
    29→            return 'File exceeds 50MB limit';
    30→        }
    31→        return null;
    32→    }, []);
    33→
    34→    const handleFileSelect = useCallback(
    35→        (file: File) => {
    36→            const validationError = validateFile(file);
    37→            if (validationError) {
    38→                setError(validationError);
    39→                setSelectedFile(null);
    40→                return;
    41→            }
    42→            setError(null);
    43→            setSelectedFile(file);
    44→        },
    45→        [validateFile]
    46→    );
    47→
    48→    const handleInputChange = useCallback(
    49→        (e: React.ChangeEvent<HTMLInputElement>) => {
    50→            const file = e.target.files?.[0];
    51→            if (file) {
    52→                handleFileSelect(file);
    53→            }
    54→        },
    55→        [handleFileSelect]
    56→    );
    57→
    58→    const handleDragOver = useCallback((e: DragEvent) => {
    59→        e.preventDefault();
    60→        setDragOver(true);
    61→    }, []);
    62→
    63→    const handleDragLeave = useCallback((e: DragEvent) => {
    64→        e.preventDefault();
    65→        setDragOver(false);
    66→    }, []);
    67→
    68→    const handleDrop = useCallback(
    69→        (e: DragEvent) => {
    70→            e.preventDefault();
    71→            setDragOver(false);
    72→            const file = e.dataTransfer.files[0];
    73→            if (file) {
    74→                handleFileSelect(file);
    75→            }
    76→        },
    77→        [handleFileSelect]
    78→    );
    79→
    80→    const handleUpload = useCallback(async () => {
    81→        if (!selectedFile) return;
    82→
    83→        try {
    84→            await uploadMutation.mutateAsync({ file: selectedFile });
    85→            // Reset and close on success
    86→            setSelectedFile(null);
    87→            setError(null);
    88→            onOpenChange(false);
    89→        } catch (err) {
    90→            setError(err instanceof Error ? err.message : 'Upload failed');
    91→        }
    92→    }, [selectedFile, uploadMutation, onOpenChange]);
    93→
    94→    const handleOpenChange = useCallback(
    95→        (nextOpen: boolean) => {
    96→            if (!nextOpen) {
    97→                setSelectedFile(null);
    98→                setError(null);
    99→            }
   100→            onOpenChange(nextOpen);
   101→        },
   102→        [onOpenChange]
   103→    );
   104→
   105→    const formatFileSize = (bytes: number): string => {
   106→        if (bytes < 1024) return `${bytes} B`;
   107→        if (bytes < 1024 * 1024) return `${(bytes / 1024).toFixed(1)} KB`;
   108→        return `${(bytes / (1024 * 1024)).toFixed(1)} MB`;
   109→    };
   110→
   111→    return (
   112→        <Dialog.Root open={open} onOpenChange={handleOpenChange}>
   113→            <Dialog.Portal>
   114→                <Dialog.Overlay className="fixed inset-0 bg-black/50 data-[state=open]:animate-in data-[state=closed]:animate-out data-[state=closed]:fade-out-0 data-[state=open]:fade-in-0" />
   115→                <Dialog.Content className="fixed left-1/2 top-1/2 w-full max-w-lg -translate-x-1/2 -translate-y-1/2 rounded-lg border bg-background p-6 shadow-lg">
   116→                    <div className="flex items-center justify-between mb-4">
   117→                        <Dialog.Title className="text-lg font-semibold text-foreground">
   118→                            Upload Protocol
   119→                        </Dialog.Title>
   120→                        <Dialog.Close asChild>
   121→                            <button
   122→                                type="button"
   123→                                className="rounded-sm opacity-70 ring-offset-background transition-opacity hover:opacity-100 focus:outline-none focus:ring-2 focus:ring-ring focus:ring-offset-2"
   124→                                aria-label="Close"
   125→                            >
   126→                                <X className="h-4 w-4" />
   127→                            </button>
   128→                        </Dialog.Close>
   129→                    </div>
   130→
   131→                    <Dialog.Description className="text-sm text-muted-foreground mb-4">
   132→                        Upload a clinical trial protocol PDF for analysis. Maximum file size: 50MB.
   133→                    </Dialog.Description>
   134→
   135→                    {/* Drop zone */}
   136→                    <button
   137→                        type="button"
   138→                        className={cn(
   139→                            'flex w-full flex-col items-center justify-center rounded-lg border-2 border-dashed p-8 transition-colors cursor-pointer',
   140→                            dragOver
   141→                                ? 'border-primary bg-primary/5'
   142→                                : 'border-muted-foreground/25 hover:border-muted-foreground/50',
   143→                            selectedFile && 'border-green-500 bg-green-500/5'
   144→                        )}
   145→                        onDragOver={handleDragOver}
   146→                        onDragLeave={handleDragLeave}
   147→                        onDrop={handleDrop}
   148→                        onClick={() => fileInputRef.current?.click()}
   149→                    >
   150→                        <input
   151→                            ref={fileInputRef}
   152→                            type="file"
   153→                            accept="application/pdf"
   154→                            onChange={handleInputChange}
   155→                            className="hidden"
   156→                        />
   157→
   158→                        {selectedFile ? (
   159→                            <div className="text-center">
   160→                                <Upload className="mx-auto h-8 w-8 text-green-500 mb-2" />
   161→                                <p className="text-sm font-medium text-foreground">
   162→                                    {selectedFile.name}
   163→                                </p>
   164→                                <p className="text-xs text-muted-foreground mt-1">
   165→                                    {formatFileSize(selectedFile.size)}
   166→                                </p>
   167→                            </div>
   168→                        ) : (
   169→                            <div className="text-center">
   170→                                <Upload className="mx-auto h-8 w-8 text-muted-foreground mb-2" />
   171→                                <p className="text-sm text-muted-foreground">
   172→                                    Drop PDF here or click to browse
   173→                                </p>
   174→                                <p className="text-xs text-muted-foreground mt-1">
   175→                                    PDF files up to 50MB
   176→                                </p>
   177→                            </div>
   178→                        )}
   179→                    </button>
   180→
   181→                    {/* Error message */}
   182→                    {error && (
   183→                        <p className="mt-3 text-sm text-destructive" role="alert">
   184→                            {error}
   185→                        </p>
   186→                    )}
   187→
   188→                    {/* Actions */}
   189→                    <div className="mt-6 flex justify-end gap-3">
   190→                        <Dialog.Close asChild>
   191→                            <Button variant="outline" disabled={uploadMutation.isPending}>
   192→                                Cancel
   193→                            </Button>
   194→                        </Dialog.Close>
   195→                        <Button

   202→                                    Uploading...
   203→                                </>
   204→                            ) : (
   205→                                'Upload'
   206→                            )}
   207→                        </Button>

     3→import { useAuthStore } from '../stores/authStore';
     4→
     5→const API_BASE_URL = import.meta.env.VITE_API_URL || 'http://localhost:8000';
     6→
     7→async function fetchApi<T>(endpoint: string, options?: RequestInit): Promise<T> {
     8→    // Get auth token from store
     9→    const token = useAuthStore.getState().token;
    10→    const headers: HeadersInit = {
    11→        'Content-Type': 'application/json',
    12→        ...(token ? { Authorization: `Bearer ${token}` } : {}),
    13→        ...(options?.headers || {}),
    14→    };
    15→
    16→    const response = await fetch(`${API_BASE_URL}${endpoint}`, {
    17→        ...options,
    18→        headers,
    19→    });
    20→
    21→    if (response.status === 401) {
    22→        useAuthStore.getState().logout();
    23→        throw new Error('Session expired');
    24→    }
    25→
    26→    if (!response.ok) {
    27→        throw new Error(`API Error: ${response.status} ${response.statusText}`);
    28→    }
    29→
    30→    return response.json();
    31→}
    32→
    33→// --- TypeScript interfaces ---
    34→
    35→export interface Protocol {
    36→    id: string;
    37→    title: string;
    38→    file_uri: string;
    39→    status:
    40→        | 'uploaded'
    41→        | 'extracting'
    42→        | 'extraction_failed'
    43→        | 'grounding'
    44→        | 'grounding_failed'
    45→        | 'pending_review'
    46→        | 'processing'
    47→        | 'complete'
    48→        | 'dead_letter'
    49→        | 'archived';
    50→    error_reason: string | null;
    51→    page_count: number | null;
    52→    quality_score: number | null;
    53→    metadata_: Record<string, unknown>;
    54→    created_at: string;
    55→    updated_at: string;
    56→    version_count?: number;  // Present when deduplicate=true
    57→}
    58→
    59→export interface ProtocolListResponse {
    60→    items: Protocol[];
    61→    total: number;
    62→    page: number;
    63→    page_size: number;
    64→    pages: number;
    65→}
    66→
    67→export interface UploadResponse {
    68→    protocol_id: string;
    69→    upload_url: string;
    70→    gcs_path: string;
    71→}
    72→
    73→// --- Hooks ---
    74→
    75→export function useProtocolList(page: number, pageSize: number, status?: string, deduplicate?: boolean) {
    76→    const params = new URLSearchParams({
    77→        page: String(page),
    78→        page_size: String(pageSize),
    79→    });
    80→    if (status) {
    81→        params.set('status', status);
    82→    }
    83→    if (deduplicate) {
    84→        params.set('deduplicate', 'true');
    85→    }
    86→
    87→    return useQuery({
    88→        queryKey: ['protocols', page, pageSize, status, deduplicate],
    89→        queryFn: () => fetchApi<ProtocolListResponse>(`/protocols?${params.toString()}`),
    90→    });
    91→}
    92→
    93→export function useProtocol(
    94→    id: string,
    95→    options?: Partial<UseQueryOptions<Protocol, Error>>
    96→) {
    97→    return useQuery({
    98→        queryKey: ['protocols', id],
    99→        queryFn: () => fetchApi<Protocol>(`/protocols/${id}`),
   100→        enabled: Boolean(id),
   101→        ...options,
   102→    });
   103→}
   104→
   105→export function useUploadProtocol() {
   106→    const queryClient = useQueryClient();
   107→
   108→    return useMutation({
   109→        mutationFn: async ({ file }: { file: File }) => {
   110→            // Step 1: Get signed upload URL from backend
   111→            const uploadResp = await fetchApi<UploadResponse>('/protocols/upload', {
   112→                method: 'POST',
   113→                body: JSON.stringify({
   114→                    filename: file.name,
   115→                    content_type: file.type,
   116→                    file_size_bytes: file.size,
   117→                }),
   118→            });
   119→
   120→            // Step 2: Upload file directly to GCS via signed URL
   121→            const putResp = await fetch(uploadResp.upload_url, {
   122→                method: 'PUT',
   123→                headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/pdf' },
   124→                body: file,
   125→            });
   126→
   127→            if (!putResp.ok) {
   128→                throw new Error(`Upload failed: ${putResp.status} ${putResp.statusText}`);
   129→            }
   130→
   131→            // Step 3: Read file as base64 for quality scoring, then confirm
   132→            const arrayBuffer = await file.arrayBuffer();
   133→            const bytes = new Uint8Array(arrayBuffer);
   134→            let binary = '';
   135→            for (let i = 0; i < bytes.length; i++) {
   136→                binary += String.fromCharCode(bytes[i]);
   137→            }
   138→            const pdfBase64 = btoa(binary);
   139→
   140→            await fetchApi(`/protocols/${uploadResp.protocol_id}/confirm-upload`, {
   141→                method: 'POST',
   142→                body: JSON.stringify({ pdf_bytes_base64: pdfBase64 }),
   143→            });
   144→
   145→            return uploadResp;
   146→        },
   147→        onSuccess: () => {
   148→            queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['protocols'] });
   149→        },
   150→    });
   151→}
   152→
   153→export function useConfirmUpload() {
   154→    const queryClient = useQueryClient();
   155→
   156→    return useMutation({
   157→        mutationFn: ({ id, pdfBase64 }: { id: string; pdfBase64?: string }) =>
   158→            fetchApi(`/protocols/${id}/confirm-upload`, {
   159→                method: 'POST',
   160→                body: JSON.stringify(pdfBase64 ? { pdf_bytes_base64: pdfBase64 } : {}),
   161→            }),
   162→        onSuccess: () => {
   163→            queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['protocols'] });
   164→        },
   165→    });
   166→}
   167→
   168→export function useRetryProtocol() {
   169→    const queryClient = useQueryClient();
   170→
   171→    return useMutation({
   172→        mutationFn: (protocolId: string) =>
   173→            fetchApi<{ status: string; protocol_id: string }>(`/protocols/${protocolId}/retry`, {
   174→                method: 'POST',
   175→            }),
   176→        onSuccess: () => {
   177→            queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['protocols'] });
   178→        },
   179→    });
   180→}
   181→
   182→export interface ReExtractResponse {
   183→    status: string;
   184→    protocol_id: string;
   185→    archived_batches: number;
   186→}
   187→
   188→export function useReExtractProtocol() {
   189→    const queryClient = useQueryClient();
   190→
   191→    return useMutation({
   192→        mutationFn: (protocolId: string) =>
   193→            fetchApi<ReExtractResponse>(`/protocols/${protocolId}/re-extract`, {
   194→                method: 'POST',
   195→            }),
   196→        onSuccess: () => {
   197→            queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['protocols'] });
   198→            queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['review-batches'] });
   199→        },
   200→    });
   201→}
   202→
   203→export function useArchiveProtocol() {
   204→    const queryClient = useQueryClient();
   205→    return useMutation({
   206→        mutationFn: (protocolId: string) =>
   207→            fetchApi<Protocol>(`/protocols/${protocolId}/archive`, {
   208→                method: 'POST',
   209→            }),
   210→        onSuccess: () => {
   211→            queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['protocols'] });
   212→        },
   213→    });
   214→}
   215→

1. `POST /protocols/upload` → get upload URL (this fires the event prematurely)
2. `PUT` file to the upload URL 
3. `POST /protocols/{id}/confirm-upload` → confirm

So there's already a `confirm-upload` step. Let me check what that does and where the event should actually be fired.

   219→def confirm_upload(
   220→    protocol_id: str,
   221→    body: ConfirmUploadRequest,
   222→    db: Session = Depends(get_db),
   223→) -> ProtocolResponse:
   224→    """Confirm a protocol upload and optionally trigger quality analysis.
   225→
   226→    Looks up the protocol by ID. If pdf_bytes_base64 is provided,
   227→    decodes the PDF bytes and computes a quality score including
   228→    text extractability, page count, and encoding type.
   229→    """
   230→    protocol = db.get(Protocol, protocol_id)
   231→    if not protocol:
   232→        raise HTTPException(
   233→            status_code=404,
   234→            detail=f"Protocol {protocol_id} not found",
   235→        )
   236→
   237→    if body.pdf_bytes_base64:
   238→        try:
   239→            pdf_bytes = base64.b64decode(body.pdf_bytes_base64)
   240→            result = compute_quality_score(pdf_bytes)
   241→
   242→            protocol.quality_score = result.overall_score
   243→            protocol.page_count = result.page_count
   244→            protocol.metadata_ = {"quality": result.model_dump()}
   245→
   246→            # Store quality metadata on GCS blob (best-effort)
   247→            try:
   248→                set_blob_metadata(
   249→                    protocol.file_uri,
   250→                    quality_result_to_metadata(result),
   251→                )
   252→            except Exception:

   255→                    protocol.file_uri,
   256→                    exc_info=True,
   257→                )
   258→        except Exception:
   259→            logger.warning(
   260→                "Failed to compute quality score for protocol %s",
   261→                protocol_id,
   262→                exc_info=True,
   263→            )
   264→

   266→    db.commit()
   267→    db.refresh(protocol)
   268→

   272→@router.post("/{protocol_id}/retry", response_model=RetryResponse)
   273→async def retry_protocol(
   274→    protocol_id: str,
   275→    db: Session = Depends(get_db),
   276→) -> RetryResponse:
   277→    """Retry a failed protocol by resuming from the last LangGraph checkpoint.
   278→
   279→    Accepts protocols in extraction_failed, grounding_failed, or dead_letter states.
   280→    Updates the protocol status to processing, then resumes the pipeline from the
   281→    last successful checkpoint (via LangGraph PostgresSaver) — skipping nodes
   282→    that already succeeded (ingest, extract, parse) and retrying from the failed node.
   283→
   284→    This does NOT create a new outbox event. The retry goes directly through
   285→    the graph using the saved checkpoint. Initial pipeline runs still use the
   286→    outbox trigger pattern (handle_protocol_uploaded).
   287→    """
   288→    protocol = db.get(Protocol, protocol_id)
   289→    if not protocol:
   290→        raise HTTPException(
   291→            status_code=404,
   292→            detail=f"Protocol {protocol_id} not found",
   293→        )
   294→
   295→    retryable_states = ["extraction_failed", "grounding_failed", "dead_letter"]

Now I have the full picture. Let me verify my hypothesis definitively.

## Hypothesis Confirmed

The flow is:

1. `POST /protocols/upload` → creates DB record + publishes `ProtocolUploaded` outbox event → **commits immediately**
2. Outbox processor (polling every 1s) picks up the event and triggers the protocol processor
3. Frontend then does `PUT` to upload the actual file bytes
4. Frontend then does `POST /protocols/{id}/confirm-upload` for quality scoring

The event fires at step 1, but the file doesn't exist until step 2-3. The outbox processor wins the race.

Now let me implement the fix. The best practice is: **move the event publication to `confirm-upload`**, since that's the point where the file is confirmed to be present.

   136→@router.post("/upload", response_model=UploadResponse)
   137→def upload_protocol(
   138→    body: UploadRequest,
   139→    db: Session = Depends(get_db),
   140→) -> UploadResponse:
   141→    """Generate a signed URL for uploading a protocol PDF.
   142→
   143→    Validates the content type (must be PDF) and file size (max 50MB),
   144→    creates a Protocol record in the database, and returns a signed
   145→    URL for direct browser upload to GCS.
   146→    """
   147→    # Validate content type
   148→    if body.content_type != "application/pdf":
   149→        raise HTTPException(
   150→            status_code=400,
   151→            detail="Only PDF files are accepted",
   152→        )
   153→
   154→    # Validate file size (50MB limit)
   155→    max_size = 50 * 1024 * 1024
   156→    if body.file_size_bytes > max_size:
   157→        raise HTTPException(
   158→            status_code=400,
   159→            detail="File size exceeds 50MB limit",
   160→        )
   161→
   162→    # Generate signed upload URL
   163→    signed_url, gcs_path = generate_upload_url(
   164→        filename=body.filename,
   165→        content_type=body.content_type,
   166→    )
   167→
   168→    # Create protocol record with title from filename (strip .pdf)
   169→    title = body.filename
   170→    if title.lower().endswith(".pdf"):
   171→        title = title[:-4]
   172→
   173→    protocol = Protocol(
   174→        title=title,
   175→        file_uri=gcs_path,
   176→        status="uploaded",
   177→    )
   178→
   179→    # Persist protocol + outbox event atomically
   180→    persist_with_outbox(
   181→        session=db,
   182→        entity=protocol,
   183→        event_type=DomainEventKind.PROTOCOL_UPLOADED,
   184→        aggregate_type="protocol",
   185→        aggregate_id=protocol.id,
   186→        payload={
   187→            "protocol_id": protocol.id,
   188→            "title": title,
   189→            "file_uri": gcs_path,
   190→        },
   191→    )
   192→    db.commit()
   193→
   194→    # Check circuit breaker states — if critical services are down,
   195→    # mark protocol as pending with warning (per locked decision)
   196→    processing_warning: str | None = None
   197→    if gemini_breaker.current_state != "closed":
   198→        protocol.status = "pending"
   199→        processing_warning = "Processing delayed — AI service temporarily unavailable"
   200→        protocol.metadata_ = {
   201→            **protocol.metadata_,
   202→            "processing_warning": processing_warning,
   203→        }
   204→        db.add(protocol)
   205→        db.commit()
   206→
   207→    return UploadResponse(
   208→        protocol_id=protocol.id,
   209→        upload_url=signed_url,
   210→        gcs_path=gcs_path,
   211→        warning=processing_warning,
   212→    )
   213→

Now I have everything I need. The fix is clear:

1. **Remove** the `persist_with_outbox` from `upload_protocol` — just save the protocol record
2. **Add** the `persist_with_outbox` to `confirm_upload` — fire the event only after the file is confirmed present

     1→"""Unified outbox event handler for ProtocolUploaded events.
     2→
     3→Replaces the two-service trigger pattern (extraction_service + grounding_service)
     4→with a single consolidated handler that invokes the full 5-node pipeline:
     5→ingest -> extract -> parse -> ground -> persist
     6→
     7→Per user decision (v2.0): "Remove criteria_extracted outbox, retain protocol_uploaded"
     8→
     9→The handler is synchronous (called by OutboxProcessor.poll_and_process) and
    10→bridges to the async graph via asyncio.run(). This works without event loop
    11→conflicts because the outbox processor runs handlers in a thread executor
    12→via run_in_executor, so there is no existing event loop in the current thread.
    13→
    14→Checkpointing: Each invocation generates a unique thread_id (protocol_id:uuid4)
    15→and stores it in protocol.metadata_ so that retry_from_checkpoint can look it up.
    16→This prevents checkpoint collision when re-extracting the same protocol.
    17→"""
    18→
    19→from __future__ import annotations
    20→
    21→import asyncio
    22→import logging
    23→import os
    24→from typing import Any
    25→from uuid import uuid4
    26→
    27→from api_service.storage import engine
    28→from shared.models import Protocol
    29→from sqlmodel import Session
    30→
    31→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    32→
    33→
    34→def _cleanup_orphan_traces() -> None:
    35→    """Close stale IN_PROGRESS MLflow traces from previous crashed runs.
    36→
    37→    Runs once at module import (service startup). Idempotent -- safe to
    38→    call multiple times. Only closes traces older than 1 hour to avoid
    39→    closing currently-running pipelines.
    40→    """
    41→    import time as _time
    42→
    43→    tracking_uri = os.getenv("MLFLOW_TRACKING_URI")
    44→    if not tracking_uri:
    45→        return
    46→    try:
    47→        import mlflow
    48→
    49→        client = mlflow.MlflowClient()
    50→        cutoff_ms = int((_time.time() - 3600) * 1000)  # 1 hour ago
    51→
    52→        # MLflow 3.x: search for traces with IN_PROGRESS status
    53→        try:
    54→            traces = client.search_traces(
    55→                experiment_ids=["0"],  # Default experiment
    56→                filter_string="status = 'IN_PROGRESS'",
    57→                max_results=50,
    58→            )
    59→        except (AttributeError, TypeError):
    60→            # search_traces may not exist or have different signature
    61→            logger.debug("MLflow search_traces API not available for orphan cleanup")
    62→            return
    63→
    64→        closed = 0
    65→        for trace in traces:
    66→            trace_ts = getattr(trace.info, "timestamp_ms", None) or 0
    67→            if trace_ts < cutoff_ms:
    68→                try:
    69→                    client.end_trace(
    70→                        request_id=trace.info.request_id,
    71→                        status="ERROR",
    72→                    )
    73→                    closed += 1
    74→                except Exception:
    75→                    pass  # Trace may have already been closed
    76→        if closed:
    77→            logger.info(
    78→                "Startup orphan cleanup: closed %d stale IN_PROGRESS trace(s)",
    79→                closed,
    80→            )
    81→    except Exception:
    82→        logger.debug("Orphan trace cleanup skipped (non-fatal)", exc_info=True)
    83→
    84→
    85→# Run once at startup to clean up orphaned traces from previous crashes
    86→_cleanup_orphan_traces()
    87→
    88→
    89→def _ensure_mlflow() -> bool:
    90→    """Ensure MLflow tracking is configured in the current thread.
    91→
    92→    Returns True if MLflow tracing is available and configured.
    93→    """
    94→    try:
    95→        import mlflow
    96→
    97→        tracking_uri = os.getenv("MLFLOW_TRACKING_URI")
    98→        if tracking_uri:
    99→            mlflow.set_tracking_uri(tracking_uri)
   100→            mlflow.set_experiment("protocol-processing")
   101→            return True
   102→    except ImportError:
   103→        pass
   104→    return False
   105→
   106→
   107→def _categorize_pipeline_error(e: Exception) -> str:
   108→    """Convert exception to human-readable pipeline error reason.
   109→
   110→    Combines extraction and grounding error categorization from both
   111→    the old extraction_service and grounding_service triggers.
   112→
   113→    Args:
   114→        e: The exception that occurred during pipeline execution.
   115→
   116→    Returns:
   117→        Human-readable error message for the user.
   118→    """
   119→    error_str = str(e).lower()
   120→
   121→    # PDF / extraction errors
   122→    if "pdf" in error_str or "pymupdf" in error_str:
   123→        return "PDF text quality too low or file corrupted"
   124→    if "gcs" in error_str or "storage" in error_str or "bucket" in error_str:
   125→        return "File storage service unavailable"
   126→
   127→    # Auth / credential errors
   128→    if "credential" in error_str or "auth" in error_str:
   129→        return "Service authentication failed"
   130→
   131→    # UMLS / grounding errors
   132→    if "mcp" in error_str or "subprocess" in error_str:
   133→        return "UMLS grounding service unavailable"
   134→    if "concept_linking" in error_str or "concept_search" in error_str:
   135→        return "UMLS terminology service unavailable"
   136→
   137→    # Generic transient errors
   138→    if "circuit" in error_str:
   139→        return "AI service temporarily unavailable"
   140→    if "timeout" in error_str or "timed out" in error_str:
   141→        return "Processing timed out"
   142→    if "parse" in error_str:
   143→        return "Protocol parsing failed"
   144→
   145→    return f"Pipeline failed: {type(e).__name__}"
   146→
   147→
   148→def _update_protocol_failed(
   149→    protocol_id: str,
   150→    reason: str,
   151→    error_category: str,
   152→    exception_type: str,
   153→) -> None:
   154→    """Update protocol status to extraction_failed with error metadata.
   155→
   156→    Args:
   157→        protocol_id: Protocol ID to update.
   158→        reason: Human-readable error reason.
   159→        error_category: Short category string for the error type.
   160→        exception_type: Python exception class name.
   161→    """
   162→    try:
   163→        with Session(engine) as session:
   164→            protocol = session.get(Protocol, protocol_id)
   165→            if protocol:
   166→                protocol.status = "extraction_failed"
   167→                protocol.error_reason = reason
   168→                protocol.metadata_ = {
   169→                    **protocol.metadata_,
   170→                    "error": {
   171→                        "category": error_category,
   172→                        "reason": reason,
   173→                        "exception_type": exception_type,
   174→                    },
   175→                }
   176→                session.add(protocol)
   177→                session.commit()
   178→    except Exception:
   179→        logger.exception(
   180→            "Failed to update protocol %s status to extraction_failed",
   181→            protocol_id,
   182→        )
   183→
   184→
   185→# KNOWN LIMITATION: MLflow ContextVar async boundary (Issue #8)
   186→#
   187→# MLflow's trace context uses Python ContextVars which do NOT propagate
   188→# across asyncio task boundaries created by asyncio.create_task() or
   189→# asyncio.gather(). This means:
   190→#   - The parent trace in trigger.py correctly wraps the full pipeline run
   191→#   - Child spans created inside LangGraph nodes are attached to the parent
   192→#     trace because LangGraph uses `await` (not create_task) for node calls
   193→#   - However, if future code uses asyncio.gather() for parallel node
   194→#     execution, child spans may detach from the parent trace
   195→#   - Workaround: Pass trace context explicitly via PipelineState if parallel
   196→#     node execution is added
   197→#
   198→# This is a known MLflow limitation, not a bug in our code.
   199→
   200→
   201→async def _run_pipeline(
   202→    graph: Any,
   203→    initial_state: dict[str, Any],
   204→    config: dict[str, Any],
   205→    payload: dict[str, Any],
   206→) -> dict[str, Any]:
   207→    """Run the pipeline graph with MLflow tracing inside the async context.
   208→
   209→    MLflow ContextVars must be created in the same async context as the
   210→    LangGraph invocation. asyncio.run() creates an isolated context, so
   211→    we initialize MLflow tracing HERE (inside asyncio.run) rather than
   212→    in the sync caller.
   213→    """
   214→    if _ensure_mlflow():
   215→        import mlflow
   216→
   217→        mlflow.langchain.autolog()
   218→
   219→        with mlflow.start_span(
   220→            name="protocol_pipeline",
   221→            span_type="CHAIN",
   222→        ) as span:
   223→            # Capture trace_id immediately so finally block can force-close
   224→            # orphaned traces on process-kill or unexpected exception.
   225→            trace_id = mlflow.get_active_trace_id()
   226→            span.set_inputs(
   227→                {
   228→                    "protocol_id": payload.get("protocol_id", ""),
   229→                    "title": payload.get("title", ""),
   230→                    "file_uri": payload.get("file_uri", ""),
   231→                }
   232→            )
   233→            succeeded = False
   234→            try:
   235→                result = await graph.ainvoke(initial_state, config)
   236→                span.set_outputs(
   237→                    {
   238→                        "status": result.get("status"),
   239→                        "error": result.get("error"),
   240→                        "error_count": len(result.get("errors", [])),
   241→                    }
   242→                )
   243→                succeeded = True
   244→                return result
   245→            finally:
   246→                # Only force-close as ERROR on failure — let the context
   247→                # manager's __exit__ handle successful traces naturally.
   248→                if trace_id and not succeeded:
   249→                    logger.warning(
   250→                        "Force-closing MLflow trace %s as ERROR (pipeline failed)",
   251→                        trace_id,
   252→                    )
   253→                    try:
   254→                        mlflow.MlflowClient().end_trace(trace_id, status="ERROR")
   255→                    except Exception:
   256→                        logger.warning(
   257→                            "Could not force-close MLflow trace %s"
   258→                            " — already closed or unavailable",
   259→                            trace_id,
   260→                        )
   261→
   262→    return await graph.ainvoke(initial_state, config)
   263→
   264→
   265→def handle_protocol_uploaded(payload: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
   266→    """Handle a ProtocolUploaded event by running the full pipeline.
   267→
   268→    Constructs the initial PipelineState from the event payload and
   269→    invokes the consolidated 5-node LangGraph workflow via asyncio.run().
   270→
   271→    Uses protocol_id as the LangGraph thread_id so that retry_from_checkpoint
   272→    can locate the saved checkpoint by protocol_id alone.
   273→
   274→    Replaces both extraction_service.trigger.handle_protocol_uploaded and
   275→    grounding_service.trigger.handle_criteria_extracted from the v1.x
   276→    two-service architecture.
   277→
   278→    Args:
   279→        payload: Event payload dict containing protocol_id, file_uri, and title.
   280→
   281→    Raises:
   282→        Exception: Re-raised after logging to let the outbox processor
   283→            mark the event as failed for retry.
   284→    """
   285→    protocol_id = payload.get("protocol_id", "unknown")
   286→    logger.info(
   287→        "Handling ProtocolUploaded event for protocol %s (consolidated pipeline)",
   288→        protocol_id,
   289→    )
   290→
   291→    try:
   292→        # Generate unique thread_id per pipeline run to prevent checkpoint collision
   293→        # on re-extraction (same protocol_id would resume old completed checkpoint)
   294→        thread_id = f"{protocol_id}:{uuid4()}"
   295→
   296→        initial_state: dict[str, Any] = {
   297→            "protocol_id": payload["protocol_id"],
   298→            "file_uri": payload["file_uri"],
   299→            "title": payload["title"],
   300→            "batch_id": None,
   301→            "pdf_bytes": None,
   302→            "extraction_json": None,
   303→            "entities_json": None,
   304→            "grounded_entities_json": None,
   305→            "archived_reviewed_criteria": payload.get("archived_reviewed_criteria"),
   306→            "status": "processing",
   307→            "error": None,
   308→            "errors": [],
   309→        }
   310→
   311→        # Store thread_id in protocol metadata for retry_from_checkpoint lookup
   312→        try:
   313→            with Session(engine) as session:
   314→                protocol = session.get(Protocol, protocol_id)
   315→                if protocol:
   316→                    meta = protocol.metadata_ or {}
   317→                    protocol.metadata_ = {
   318→                        **meta,
   319→                        "pipeline_thread_id": thread_id,
   320→                    }
   321→                    session.add(protocol)
   322→                    session.commit()
   323→        except Exception:
   324→            logger.warning(
   325→                "Failed to store pipeline_thread_id for protocol %s",
   326→                protocol_id,
   327→            )
   328→
   329→        # Lazy import to avoid circular imports at module load time
   330→        from protocol_processor.graph import get_graph
   331→
   332→        graph = get_graph()
   333→
   334→        config = {"configurable": {"thread_id": thread_id}}
   335→
   336→        asyncio.run(_run_pipeline(graph, initial_state, config, payload))
   337→
   338→        logger.info(
   339→            "Protocol pipeline completed for protocol %s",
   340→            protocol_id,
   341→        )
   342→
   343→    except Exception as e:
   344→        logger.exception(
   345→            "Protocol pipeline failed for protocol %s",
   346→            protocol_id,
   347→        )
   348→        reason = _categorize_pipeline_error(e)
   349→        _update_protocol_failed(
   350→            protocol_id,
   351→            reason,
   352→            "pipeline_failed",
   353→            type(e).__name__,
   354→        )
   355→        raise
   356→
   357→
   358→async def retry_from_checkpoint(protocol_id: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
   359→    """Resume pipeline from last checkpoint for a failed protocol.
   360→
   361→    Passes None as the input state to graph.ainvoke, which tells LangGraph
   362→    to resume from the last saved checkpoint for the given thread_id instead
   363→    of starting from scratch. Reads thread_id from protocol.metadata_ where
   364→    it was stored during the original pipeline invocation.
   365→
   366→    Args:
   367→        protocol_id: Protocol ID — used to look up thread_id from metadata.
   368→
   369→    Returns:
   370→        Final pipeline state dict after resuming from checkpoint.
   371→    """
   372→    with Session(engine) as session:
   373→        protocol = session.get(Protocol, protocol_id)
   374→        if not protocol:
   375→            raise ValueError(f"Protocol {protocol_id} not found")
   376→        thread_id = (protocol.metadata_ or {}).get("pipeline_thread_id", protocol_id)
   377→
   378→    from protocol_processor.graph import get_graph
   379→
   380→    graph = get_graph()
   381→    config = {"configurable": {"thread_id": thread_id}}
   382→    # Pass None as input — LangGraph resumes from last checkpoint
   383→    result = await graph.ainvoke(None, config)
   384→    return result
   385→
```

> AGENT

Clean. Here's a summary of the fix:

## Root Cause

The upload is a 3-step process:
1. **`POST /protocols/upload`** — creates the DB record and returns the upload URL
2. **`PUT` to upload URL** — client uploads the actual PDF bytes
3. **`POST /protocols/{id}/confirm-upload`** — confirms the upload, does quality scoring

The bug: the `ProtocolUploaded` outbox event was published in step 1, but the PDF file doesn't exist until step 2. The outbox processor (polling every 1s) picks up the event and the protocol processor tries to read a file that hasn't been uploaded yet.

## Fix

- **Removed** the `persist_with_outbox` call from `upload_protocol` (step 1) — now it just saves the protocol record with `db.add()`
- **Added** the `persist_with_outbox` call to `confirm_upload` (step 3) — the event fires only after the file is confirmed present

This is the correct approach because `confirm-upload` is the semantic point where the upload is complete and processing should begin. No retry/delay hacks needed.

     1→"""Outbox processor for publishing pending domain events.
     2→
     3→Implements the transactional outbox pattern with at-least-once delivery.
     4→The OutboxProcessor polls the outbox table for pending events and dispatches
     5→them to registered handlers. The persist_with_outbox helper enables atomic
     6→entity + event writes in a single database transaction.
     7→"""
     8→
     9→from __future__ import annotations
    10→
    11→import asyncio
    12→import logging
    13→from datetime import datetime, timezone
    14→from typing import Any, Callable
    15→from uuid import uuid4
    16→
    17→from shared.models import OutboxEvent
    18→from sqlalchemy import Engine
    19→from sqlmodel import Session, col, select
    20→
    21→from events_py.models import DomainEventKind
    22→
    23→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    24→
    25→MAX_RETRIES = 3
    26→
    27→
    28→class OutboxProcessor:
    29→    """Processes pending outbox events with at-least-once delivery.
    30→
    31→    Usage:
    32→        processor = OutboxProcessor(engine, handlers={
    33→            "protocol_uploaded": [handle_protocol_uploaded],
    34→        })
    35→        await processor.start()  # Runs polling loop
    36→        await processor.stop()   # Graceful shutdown
    37→    """
    38→
    39→    def __init__(
    40→        self,
    41→        engine: Engine,
    42→        handlers: dict[str, list[Callable[..., Any]]] | None = None,
    43→        poll_interval: float = 1.0,
    44→        batch_size: int = 100,
    45→    ) -> None:
    46→        """Initialize the outbox processor.
    47→
    48→        Args:
    49→            engine: SQLAlchemy database engine.
    50→            handlers: Maps event_type string to handler callbacks.
    51→            poll_interval: Seconds between polls.
    52→            batch_size: Max events per poll cycle.
    53→        """
    54→        self.engine = engine
    55→        self.handlers: dict[str, list[Callable[..., Any]]] = handlers or {}
    56→        self.poll_interval = poll_interval
    57→        self.batch_size = batch_size
    58→        self._shutdown_event = asyncio.Event()
    59→
    60→    def poll_and_process(self) -> int:
    61→        """Poll for pending events and process them.
    62→
    63→        Returns:
    64→            Number of events processed in this poll cycle.
    65→        """
    66→        processed = 0
    67→        with Session(self.engine) as session:
    68→            statement = (
    69→                select(OutboxEvent)
    70→                .where(col(OutboxEvent.status).in_(["pending", "failed"]))
    71→                .order_by(OutboxEvent.created_at.asc())  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
    72→                .limit(self.batch_size)
    73→            )
    74→
    75→            # Use FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED on PostgreSQL
    76→            # for concurrent processor safety
    77→            db_url = str(self.engine.url)
    78→            if "postgresql" in db_url:
    79→                statement = statement.with_for_update(skip_locked=True)
    80→
    81→            events = session.exec(statement).all()
    82→
    83→            for event in events:
    84→                event_handlers = self.handlers.get(event.event_type, [])
    85→
    86→                if not event_handlers:
    87→                    logger.debug(
    88→                        "No handlers for event type: %s",
    89→                        event.event_type,
    90→                    )
    91→
    92→                try:
    93→                    for handler in event_handlers:
    94→                        handler(event.payload)
    95→
    96→                    event.status = "published"
    97→                    event.published_at = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
    98→                    session.add(event)
    99→                    processed += 1
   100→
   101→                    logger.info(
   102→                        "Published event %s (type=%s)",
   103→                        event.id,
   104→                        event.event_type,
   105→                    )
   106→                except Exception:
   107→                    event.retry_count += 1
   108→                    if event.retry_count >= MAX_RETRIES:
   109→                        event.status = "dead_letter"
   110→                        logger.warning(
   111→                            "Event %s exhausted retries (%d), marking as dead_letter",
   112→                            event.id,
   113→                            event.retry_count,
   114→                        )
   115→                        # Update protocol status to dead_letter if protocol event
   116→                        if event.aggregate_type == "protocol":

   118→
   119→                            protocol = session.get(Protocol, event.aggregate_id)
   120→                            if protocol:
   121→                                protocol.status = "dead_letter"
   122→                                protocol.error_reason = "Maximum retries exceeded"
   123→                                protocol.metadata_ = {
   124→                                    **protocol.metadata_,
   125→                                    "error": {
   126→                                        "category": "pipeline_failed",
   127→                                        "reason": "Maximum retries exceeded",
   128→                                        "retry_count": event.retry_count,
   129→                                    },
   130→                                }
   131→                                session.add(protocol)
   132→                    else:
   133→                        event.status = "failed"
   134→
   135→                    session.add(event)
   136→                    logger.exception(
   137→                        "Failed to process event %s (type=%s, retry_count=%d)",
   138→                        event.id,
   139→                        event.event_type,
   140→                        event.retry_count,
   141→                    )
   142→
   143→            session.commit()
   144→
   145→        return processed
   146→
   147→    async def start(self) -> None:
   148→        """Run the polling loop until stop() is called."""
   149→        logger.info(
   150→            "Outbox processor started (interval=%.1fs, batch_size=%d)",
   151→            self.poll_interval,
   152→            self.batch_size,
   153→        )
   154→        while not self._shutdown_event.is_set():
   155→            try:
   156→                count = await asyncio.get_event_loop().run_in_executor(
   157→                    None, self.poll_and_process
   158→                )
   159→                if count > 0:
   160→                    logger.info("Processed %d outbox events", count)
   161→            except Exception:
   162→                logger.exception("Error in outbox processor poll cycle")
   163→            try:
   164→                await asyncio.wait_for(
   165→                    self._shutdown_event.wait(),
   166→                    timeout=self.poll_interval,
   167→                )
   168→            except asyncio.TimeoutError:
   169→                pass
   170→        logger.info("Outbox processor stopped")
   171→
   172→    async def stop(self) -> None:
   173→        """Signal the processor to stop after current poll."""
   174→        logger.info("Outbox processor shutdown requested")
   175→        self._shutdown_event.set()
   176→
   177→
   178→def persist_with_outbox(
   179→    session: Session,
   180→    entity: Any,
   181→    event_type: DomainEventKind,
   182→    aggregate_type: str,
   183→    aggregate_id: str,
   184→    payload: dict[str, Any],
   185→    idempotency_key: str | None = None,
   186→) -> OutboxEvent:
   187→    """Persist an entity and its outbox event in the same transaction.
   188→
   189→    The caller is responsible for committing the session.
   190→
   191→    Args:
   192→        session: Active SQLModel session (not yet committed).
   193→        entity: The SQLModel entity to persist.
   194→        event_type: Domain event kind.
   195→        aggregate_type: Type of aggregate (e.g. "protocol").
   196→        aggregate_id: ID of the affected aggregate.
   197→        payload: Event payload dict.
   198→        idempotency_key: Dedup key; auto-generated if omitted.
   199→
   200→    Returns:
   201→        The created OutboxEvent (uncommitted).
   202→    """
   203→    ikey = idempotency_key or (f"{event_type.value}:{aggregate_id}:{uuid4()}")
   204→
   205→    outbox_event = OutboxEvent(
   206→        event_type=event_type.value,
   207→        aggregate_type=aggregate_type,
   208→        aggregate_id=aggregate_id,
   209→        payload=payload,
   210→        idempotency_key=ikey,
   211→        status="pending",
   212→    )
   213→
   214→    session.add(entity)
   215→    session.add(outbox_event)
   216→
   217→    return outbox_event
   218→
```

handler(event.payload)  # This is SYNCHRONOUS

The `handle_protocol_uploaded` handler runs the **entire pipeline** synchronously (including LLM calls, PDF parsing, grounding — everything). The outbox processor runs it via `run_in_executor` in a thread, and it holds the DB session open with `FOR UPDATE` locks until the handler completes (line 143: `session.commit()` only after all handlers finish).

Now let me check what database is being used — if it's SQLite, the `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` isn't used, but SQLite has its own write locking. The outbox processor's `poll_and_process` holds a session open and runs the handler (the full pipeline) inside it before committing. If the pipeline handler also opens its own session to update the protocol (as we saw in `trigger.py` line 313), and the DB is SQLite, we could have a write lock contention.

OK so the frontend calls `confirm-upload` synchronously and waits for it. And `confirm-upload` now uses `persist_with_outbox` which just writes to DB. The `db.commit()` should return immediately.

Let me check if the `confirm-upload` endpoint is actually returning by looking at the server logs. Actually, the user said "stuck uploading forever" — let me check if the issue is that `confirm-upload` is actually completing fine, but the frontend isn't detecting completion because `persist_with_outbox` might be duplicating the entity insert.

Wait — I think I see the issue. Look at line 214 of outbox.py: `session.add(entity)`. In the original `upload_protocol`, the protocol was a **new** entity not yet in the session. But in `confirm_upload`, the protocol was already fetched via `db.get()` and is already tracked by the session. When `persist_with_outbox` calls `session.add(protocol)` again, that's fine — SQLAlchemy handles it.

Let me check if the real problem is the `db.refresh(protocol)` call. If the outbox processor picked up the event instantly and is holding a `FOR UPDATE` lock on the protocol row (through its session), then `db.refresh()` might block waiting for that lock.

PostgreSQL. Now the `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` is active. But the outbox processor locks the **outbox event** row, not the protocol row. So that shouldn't block `db.refresh(protocol)`.

Let me look at this from a different angle. The outbox processor runs `handle_protocol_uploaded` which runs the entire pipeline. Inside the pipeline, `trigger.py` line 312-322 opens its own session and updates the protocol:

```python
with Session(engine) as session:
    protocol = session.get(Protocol, protocol_id)
    if protocol:
        protocol.metadata_ = {...}
        session.add(protocol)
        session.commit()
```

This happens in a separate thread (via `run_in_executor`). Meanwhile `confirm_upload`'s session might still hold a row-level lock on the protocol from the `db.get()` + `session.add()` + `db.commit()`. But no — `db.commit()` releases locks.

Actually, wait. I just realized — the `confirm_upload` endpoint is **synchronous** (`def confirm_upload`, not `async def`). In FastAPI, sync endpoints run in a thread pool. The `db.commit()` commits, the `db.refresh()` re-reads — this should all complete before returning.

Let me check if maybe the issue is the frontend isn't handling the response properly because the response shape changed. The `confirm-upload` endpoint returns `ProtocolResponse`, and the frontend isn't doing anything special with the return value — it just awaits the call.

   191→def local_save_file(blob_path: str, data: bytes) -> None:
   192→    """Save uploaded file to local storage with SHA-256 deduplication.
   193→
   194→    Uses a hash-index file (.hash-index.json) in the upload directory to
   195→    detect duplicate content. If an identical file already exists, creates a
   196→    symlink to the original rather than storing duplicate bytes.
   197→
   198→    Args:
   199→        blob_path: Relative path within the local upload directory.
   200→        data: Raw file bytes to store.
   201→    """
   202→    file_path = _LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR / blob_path
   203→    file_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   204→
   205→    content_hash = _content_hash(data)
   206→    hash_index_path = _LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR / ".hash-index.json"
   207→
   208→    # Load existing hash index or create empty dict
   209→    hash_index: dict[str, str] = {}
   210→    if hash_index_path.exists():
   211→        try:
   212→            hash_index = json.loads(hash_index_path.read_text())
   213→        except (json.JSONDecodeError, OSError):
   214→            logger.warning("Could not read hash index — starting fresh")
   215→
   216→    if content_hash in hash_index:
   217→        existing_path = _LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR / hash_index[content_hash]
   218→        logger.info(
   219→            "Dedup: content hash %s... already stored at %s",
   220→            content_hash[:8],
   221→            existing_path,
   222→        )
   223→        # Create symlink to existing file (saves disk space)
   224→        if existing_path.exists() and not file_path.exists():
   225→            file_path.symlink_to(existing_path)
   226→        elif not file_path.exists():
   227→            # Fallback: existing file gone, store normally and update index
   228→            file_path.write_bytes(data)
   229→            hash_index[content_hash] = blob_path
   230→    else:
   231→        # New content — store file and record in index
   232→        file_path.write_bytes(data)
   233→        hash_index[content_hash] = blob_path
   234→        logger.info("Local storage: saved %d bytes to %s", len(data), file_path)
   235→
   236→    # Write updated hash index back to disk
   237→    try:
   238→        hash_index_path.write_text(json.dumps(hash_index, indent=2))
   239→    except OSError:
   240→        logger.warning("Could not write hash index to %s", hash_index_path)
   241→

The file saving code looks fine. Let me think about this problem from the user's perspective. They said the upload is stuck. The spinner is tied to `uploadMutation.isPending`. This stays true until the `mutateAsync` promise resolves or rejects.

Wait — I just realized something. The `confirm-upload` response might be blocked by the outbox processor. Here's the sequence:

1. `confirm_upload` calls `persist_with_outbox` → adds protocol + outbox event to session
2. `db.commit()` → commits both, releases transaction
3. `db.refresh(protocol)` → tries to re-read protocol from DB

But between steps 2 and 3, the outbox processor (polling every 1s) could pick up the event and run `handle_protocol_uploaded`, which opens its OWN session and updates the protocol row. The `trigger.py` code (line 312-322) updates `protocol.metadata_` with `pipeline_thread_id` and commits. This is fine.

But then the pipeline runs fully (ingest node tries to read the file). At this point the file SHOULD exist because the PUT happened before confirm. So the pipeline should succeed.

     1→"""PDF quality analyzer for computing text extractability and encoding type.
     2→
     3→Analyzes uploaded protocol PDFs to determine:
     4→- Text extractability ratio (pages with extractable text / total pages)
     5→- Page count
     6→- Whether the document contains images (scanned content)
     7→- Encoding type classification (text, scanned, mixed)
     8→- Overall quality score as a weighted composite
     9→
    10→Uses PyMuPDF (fitz) for PDF analysis without requiring external tools.
    11→"""
    12→
    13→from __future__ import annotations
    14→
    15→import logging
    16→
    17→import pymupdf as fitz
    18→from pydantic import BaseModel
    19→
    20→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    21→
    22→
    23→class QualityResult(BaseModel):
    24→    """Result of PDF quality analysis.
    25→
    26→    Attributes:
    27→        text_extractability: Ratio of pages with extractable text (0.0-1.0).
    28→        page_count: Total number of pages in the PDF.
    29→        has_images: Whether any pages contain embedded images.
    30→        encoding_type: Classification -- "text", "scanned", or "mixed".
    31→        overall_score: Weighted composite quality score (0.0-1.0).
    32→        is_low_quality: True if overall_score < 0.5.
    33→    """
    34→
    35→    text_extractability: float
    36→    page_count: int
    37→    has_images: bool
    38→    encoding_type: str
    39→    overall_score: float
    40→    is_low_quality: bool
    41→
    42→
    43→def compute_quality_score(pdf_bytes: bytes) -> QualityResult:
    44→    """Analyze PDF bytes and compute a quality score.
    45→
    46→    Opens the PDF from raw bytes using PyMuPDF, examines each page
    47→    for text content and images, and produces a composite quality
    48→    score useful for determining extraction feasibility.
    49→
    50→    Args:
    51→        pdf_bytes: Raw PDF file content.
    52→
    53→    Returns:
    54→        QualityResult with all analysis fields populated.
    55→
    56→    Raises:
    57→        Exception: If the PDF cannot be opened or parsed.
    58→    """
    59→    doc = fitz.open(stream=pdf_bytes, filetype="pdf")
    60→
    61→    total_pages = len(doc)
    62→    pages_with_text = 0
    63→    pages_with_images = 0
    64→
    65→    for page in doc:
    66→        # Check for extractable text
    67→        text = page.get_text("text").strip()
    68→        if text:
    69→            pages_with_text += 1
    70→
    71→        # Check for embedded images
    72→        images = page.get_images()
    73→        if images:
    74→            pages_with_images += 1
    75→
    76→    doc.close()
    77→
    78→    # Calculate metrics
    79→    if total_pages == 0:
    80→        return QualityResult(
    81→            text_extractability=0.0,
    82→            page_count=0,
    83→            has_images=False,
    84→            encoding_type="scanned",
    85→            overall_score=0.0,
    86→            is_low_quality=True,
    87→        )
    88→
    89→    text_extractability = pages_with_text / total_pages
    90→    has_images = pages_with_images > 0
    91→
    92→    # Determine encoding type
    93→    if text_extractability > 0.8:
    94→        encoding_type = "text"
    95→    elif text_extractability < 0.2:
    96→        encoding_type = "scanned"
    97→    else:
    98→        encoding_type = "mixed"
    99→
   100→    # Calculate composite score
   101→    # - 70% weight on text extractability (most important for extraction)
   102→    # - 20% weight on page count (more pages = more content, capped at 100)
   103→    # - 10% weight on encoding type bonus
   104→    encoding_bonus = (
   105→        1.0 if encoding_type == "text" else 0.5 if encoding_type == "mixed" else 0.0
   106→    )
   107→    overall_score = (
   108→        0.7 * text_extractability
   109→        + 0.2 * min(1.0, total_pages / 100)
   110→        + 0.1 * encoding_bonus
   111→    )
   112→
   113→    is_low_quality = overall_score < 0.5
   114→
   115→    return QualityResult(
   116→        text_extractability=text_extractability,
   117→        page_count=total_pages,
   118→        has_images=has_images,
   119→        encoding_type=encoding_type,
   120→        overall_score=overall_score,
   121→        is_low_quality=is_low_quality,
   122→    )
   123→
   124→
   125→def quality_result_to_metadata(
   126→    result: QualityResult,
   127→) -> dict[str, str]:
   128→    """Convert QualityResult to flat string dict for GCS custom metadata.
   129→
   130→    GCS custom metadata values must be strings. This converts
   131→    all fields to string representations.
   132→
   133→    Args:
   134→        result: QualityResult from compute_quality_score.
   135→
   136→    Returns:
   137→        Dict with string keys and string values for GCS metadata.
   138→    """
   139→    return {
   140→        "quality_score": str(result.overall_score),
   141→        "text_extractability": str(result.text_extractability),
   142→        "page_count": str(result.page_count),
   143→        "encoding_type": result.encoding_type,
   144→        "has_images": str(result.has_images).lower(),
   145→        "is_low_quality": str(result.is_low_quality).lower(),
   146→    }
   147→

     1→"""MLflow request tracing middleware for FastAPI.
     2→
     3→Uses a pure ASGI middleware instead of Starlette's BaseHTTPMiddleware to
     4→preserve contextvars propagation (required for MLflow tracing).
     5→BaseHTTPMiddleware runs call_next in a thread pool, which breaks
     6→contextvars and produces malformed trace spans.
     7→"""
     8→
     9→import logging
    10→import os
    11→import time
    12→
    13→from starlette.types import ASGIApp, Message, Receive, Scope, Send
    14→
    15→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    16→
    17→# Skip tracing for health/readiness probes
    18→_SKIP_PATHS = {"/health", "/ready", "/"}
    19→
    20→
    21→class MLflowRequestMiddleware:
    22→    """Pure ASGI middleware that creates MLflow traces for API requests."""
    23→
    24→    def __init__(self, app: ASGIApp) -> None:
    25→        """Store the ASGI app to call."""
    26→        self.app = app
    27→
    28→    async def __call__(self, scope: Scope, receive: Receive, send: Send) -> None:
    29→        """Handle request and optionally wrap it in an MLflow span."""
    30→        if scope["type"] != "http":
    31→            await self.app(scope, receive, send)
    32→            return
    33→
    34→        path = scope.get("path", "")
    35→        if path in _SKIP_PATHS:
    36→            await self.app(scope, receive, send)
    37→            return
    38→
    39→        tracking_uri = os.getenv("MLFLOW_TRACKING_URI")
    40→        if not tracking_uri:
    41→            await self.app(scope, receive, send)
    42→            return
    43→
    44→        try:
    45→            import mlflow
    46→        except ImportError:
    47→            await self.app(scope, receive, send)
    48→            return
    49→
    50→        method = scope.get("method", "?")
    51→        start = time.perf_counter()
    52→        status_code = 500  # default until we see the actual response
    53→
    54→        # Capture status code from response headers
    55→        async def send_wrapper(message: Message) -> None:
    56→            nonlocal status_code
    57→            if message["type"] == "http.response.start":
    58→                status_code = message.get("status", 500)
    59→            await send(message)
    60→
    61→        app_called = False
    62→        try:
    63→            with mlflow.start_span(
    64→                name=f"{method} {path}",
    65→                span_type="HTTP",
    66→            ) as span:
    67→                query = scope.get("query_string", b"").decode("utf-8", errors="replace")
    68→                span.set_inputs({"method": method, "path": path, "query": query})
    69→
    70→                app_called = True
    71→                await self.app(scope, receive, send_wrapper)
    72→                latency_ms = (time.perf_counter() - start) * 1000
    73→
    74→                span.set_outputs(
    75→                    {
    76→                        "status_code": status_code,
    77→                        "latency_ms": round(latency_ms, 2),
    78→                    }
    79→                )
    80→        except Exception:
    81→            logger.debug(
    82→                "MLflow tracing failed, continuing without trace",
    83→                exc_info=True,
    84→            )
    85→            if not app_called:
    86→                await self.app(scope, receive, send)
    87→
```

The outbox processor runs as `asyncio.create_task(processor.start())` — a background async task. The `poll_and_process` is called via `run_in_executor` (line 156 of outbox.py). The handler runs in a thread pool thread.

So `confirm-upload` commits the DB → returns the response → outbox processor later picks up the event in the background. The endpoint should return immediately.

Wait. Let me re-check something. FastAPI handles sync endpoints (non-async `def`) by running them in a thread pool. And the outbox processor's `poll_and_process` also runs in the default thread pool via `run_in_executor(None, ...)`. If the thread pool is exhausted (only 1 thread?), could there be a deadlock?

Actually no — `confirm_upload` is a sync function run in a threadpool worker. It commits and returns. The response goes back to the client. Then on the next poll cycle, the outbox processor's `run_in_executor` runs `poll_and_process` in another threadpool worker. These are independent.

Hmm, let me think about this differently. Maybe the issue is that the outbox processor already polled and found no pending events (since the event didn't exist yet), and the next poll won't happen for another second. That's fine — a 1-second delay isn't "stuck forever."

OK I think I may have been overthinking this. Let me check if maybe the **real** issue is that `confirm_upload` previously did NOT use `persist_with_outbox` — it used `db.add(protocol)`. The `persist_with_outbox` function calls `session.add(entity)` which marks it as needing an INSERT. But the protocol already exists in the DB (it was created in upload_protocol). If the session doesn't know about it (since it's a different request/session), `session.add()` might try to INSERT instead of UPDATE, causing a primary key conflict that blocks or errors.

Wait — I just spotted it. `persist_with_outbox` calls `session.add(entity)` (outbox.py line 214). But the protocol was already fetched by `db.get()` and its `metadata_` was modified by the quality scoring code (line 234: `protocol.metadata_ = {"quality": result.model_dump()}`). Then `persist_with_outbox` calls `session.add(protocol)` again — this is a no-op. Then the outbox event is also added. Then `db.commit()` commits both.

Then `db.refresh(protocol)` re-reads the protocol. This should work fine...

UNLESS — the outbox processor, running in the background, already picked up the event, ran `handle_protocol_uploaded`, which opens its own session and does `session.get(Protocol, protocol_id)` then `session.add(protocol)` then `session.commit()`. If this happens between our `db.commit()` and `db.refresh()`, there could be a conflict. But PostgreSQL handles concurrent reads fine.

Hmm, you know what, let me look at this from a completely practical angle. The user might have **old outbox events** from the previous (broken) runs that are in `"pending"` or `"failed"` status. The outbox processor picks those up first (ordered by `created_at ASC`). The handler for those old events would try to fetch the PDF and fail (file doesn't exist), consuming the thread for a long time. If the thread pool only has limited workers, the new event from confirm-upload gets queued behind the failed retries.

But wait — failed events get marked `"failed"` and retry_count incremented. After 3 retries they go to `"dead_letter"`. So after a few poll cycles, old events should clear out.

Actually, the real realization is: **the old upload_protocol code created outbox events that no longer had corresponding files.** These events are now in `"failed"` status with retry_count < 3. Each poll cycle, the outbox processor retries them (because it queries `status IN ("pending", "failed")`), and each retry hangs for a while (trying to fetch the PDF, failing, running error handling, etc.). This blocks the processor.

But even so, the handler for a FileNotFoundError should fail fast (it's a synchronous file check), update the protocol to `extraction_failed`, and move on. The poll cycle processes all events in a batch and commits. So the new event should still get processed.

There it is. The server (PID 55103) is listening but **5 browser connections are ESTABLISHED** and not completing, plus my curl is stuck too. The server is completely hung — not responding to anything, including `/health`.

This means the server's event loop or thread pool is completely blocked. The outbox processor's `handle_protocol_uploaded` runs the **entire LLM pipeline synchronously** inside `run_in_executor`. If it's using the **default thread pool** (which is also used by FastAPI for sync endpoints), then a long-running pipeline execution would exhaust the thread pool and block ALL sync endpoint handlers.

   161→            except Exception:
   162→                logger.exception("Error in outbox processor poll cycle")
   163→            try:
```

> AGENT

**`run_in_executor(None, ...)`** — `None` means the **default** executor. And FastAPI/Starlette also uses the default executor for sync endpoints. The default `ThreadPoolExecutor` has a limited number of workers (typically `min(32, os.cpu_count() + 4)`).

But the real problem is worse. `poll_and_process` runs `handler(event.payload)` synchronously, which calls `handle_protocol_uploaded`, which calls `asyncio.run()` to run the full pipeline. `asyncio.run()` creates a **new event loop** in the current thread and blocks until the pipeline completes. This means one thread pool worker is occupied for the **entire duration of the pipeline** (which includes LLM calls, PDF parsing, UMLS grounding — potentially minutes).

And here's the smoking gun: there are probably old failed outbox events being retried. Each retry runs the pipeline handler, which hangs on `asyncio.run()` trying to fetch a non-existent PDF, failing, and then retrying. Multiple failed events being processed could exhaust the thread pool.

But actually, even a single pipeline run would block one thread for minutes. With a small thread pool, this could indeed block all sync endpoints.

Let me kill the hung server and restart it, then implement a proper fix:

> TOOL
