     1→"""Outbox processor for publishing pending domain events.
     2→
     3→Implements the transactional outbox pattern with at-least-once delivery.
     4→The OutboxProcessor polls the outbox table for pending events and dispatches
     5→them to registered handlers. The persist_with_outbox helper enables atomic
     6→entity + event writes in a single database transaction.
     7→"""
     8→
     9→from __future__ import annotations
    10→
    11→import asyncio
    12→import logging
    13→from datetime import datetime, timezone
    14→from typing import Any, Callable
    15→from uuid import uuid4
    16→
    17→from shared.models import OutboxEvent
    18→from sqlalchemy import Engine
    19→from sqlmodel import Session, col, select
    20→
    21→from events_py.models import DomainEventKind
    22→
    23→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    24→
    25→MAX_RETRIES = 3
    26→
    27→
    28→class OutboxProcessor:
    29→    """Processes pending outbox events with at-least-once delivery.
    30→
    31→    Usage:
    32→        processor = OutboxProcessor(engine, handlers={
    33→            "protocol_uploaded": [handle_protocol_uploaded],
    34→        })
    35→        await processor.start()  # Runs polling loop
    36→        await processor.stop()   # Graceful shutdown
    37→    """
    38→
    39→    def __init__(
    40→        self,
    41→        engine: Engine,
    42→        handlers: dict[str, list[Callable[..., Any]]] | None = None,
    43→        poll_interval: float = 1.0,
    44→        batch_size: int = 100,
    45→    ) -> None:
    46→        """Initialize the outbox processor.
    47→
    48→        Args:
    49→            engine: SQLAlchemy database engine.
    50→            handlers: Maps event_type string to handler callbacks.
    51→            poll_interval: Seconds between polls.
    52→            batch_size: Max events per poll cycle.
    53→        """
    54→        self.engine = engine
    55→        self.handlers: dict[str, list[Callable[..., Any]]] = handlers or {}
    56→        self.poll_interval = poll_interval
    57→        self.batch_size = batch_size
    58→        self._shutdown_event = asyncio.Event()
    59→
    60→    def poll_and_process(self) -> int:
    61→        """Poll for pending events and process them.
    62→
    63→        Returns:
    64→            Number of events processed in this poll cycle.
    65→        """
    66→        processed = 0
    67→        with Session(self.engine) as session:
    68→            statement = (
    69→                select(OutboxEvent)
    70→                .where(col(OutboxEvent.status).in_(["pending", "failed"]))
    71→                .order_by(OutboxEvent.created_at.asc())  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
    72→                .limit(self.batch_size)
    73→            )
    74→
    75→            # Use FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED on PostgreSQL
    76→            # for concurrent processor safety
    77→            db_url = str(self.engine.url)
    78→            if "postgresql" in db_url:
    79→                statement = statement.with_for_update(skip_locked=True)
    80→
    81→            events = session.exec(statement).all()
    82→
    83→            for event in events:
    84→                event_handlers = self.handlers.get(event.event_type, [])
    85→
    86→                if not event_handlers:
    87→                    logger.debug(
    88→                        "No handlers for event type: %s",
    89→                        event.event_type,
    90→                    )
    91→
    92→                try:
    93→                    for handler in event_handlers:
    94→                        handler(event.payload)
    95→
    96→                    event.status = "published"
    97→                    event.published_at = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
    98→                    session.add(event)
    99→                    processed += 1
   100→
   101→                    logger.info(
   102→                        "Published event %s (type=%s)",
   103→                        event.id,
   104→                        event.event_type,
   105→                    )
   106→                except Exception:
   107→                    event.retry_count += 1
   108→                    if event.retry_count >= MAX_RETRIES:
   109→                        event.status = "dead_letter"
   110→                        logger.warning(
   111→                            "Event %s exhausted retries (%d), marking as dead_letter",
   112→                            event.id,
   113→                            event.retry_count,
   114→                        )
   115→                        # Update protocol status to dead_letter if protocol event
   116→                        if event.aggregate_type == "protocol":
   117→                            from shared.models import Protocol
   118→
   119→                            protocol = session.get(Protocol, event.aggregate_id)
   120→                            if protocol:
   121→                                protocol.status = "dead_letter"
   122→                                protocol.error_reason = "Maximum retries exceeded"
   123→                                protocol.metadata_ = {
   124→                                    **protocol.metadata_,
   125→                                    "error": {
   126→                                        "category": "pipeline_failed",
   127→                                        "reason": "Maximum retries exceeded",
   128→                                        "retry_count": event.retry_count,
   129→                                    },
   130→                                }
   131→                                session.add(protocol)
   132→                    else:
   133→                        event.status = "failed"
   134→
   135→                    session.add(event)
   136→                    logger.exception(
   137→                        "Failed to process event %s (type=%s, retry_count=%d)",
   138→                        event.id,
   139→                        event.event_type,
   140→                        event.retry_count,
   141→                    )
   142→
   143→            session.commit()
   144→
   145→        return processed
   146→
   147→    async def start(self) -> None:
   148→        """Run the polling loop until stop() is called."""
   149→        logger.info(
   150→            "Outbox processor started (interval=%.1fs, batch_size=%d)",
   151→            self.poll_interval,
   152→            self.batch_size,
   153→        )
   154→        while not self._shutdown_event.is_set():
   155→            try:
   156→                count = await asyncio.get_event_loop().run_in_executor(
   157→                    None, self.poll_and_process
   158→                )
   159→                if count > 0:
   160→                    logger.info("Processed %d outbox events", count)
   161→            except Exception:
   162→                logger.exception("Error in outbox processor poll cycle")
   163→            try:
   164→                await asyncio.wait_for(
   165→                    self._shutdown_event.wait(),
   166→                    timeout=self.poll_interval,
   167→                )
   168→            except asyncio.TimeoutError:
   169→                pass
   170→        logger.info("Outbox processor stopped")
   171→
   172→    async def stop(self) -> None:
   173→        """Signal the processor to stop after current poll."""
   174→        logger.info("Outbox processor shutdown requested")
   175→        self._shutdown_event.set()
   176→
   177→
   178→def persist_with_outbox(
   179→    session: Session,
   180→    entity: Any,
   181→    event_type: DomainEventKind,
   182→    aggregate_type: str,
   183→    aggregate_id: str,
   184→    payload: dict[str, Any],
   185→    idempotency_key: str | None = None,
   186→) -> OutboxEvent:
   187→    """Persist an entity and its outbox event in the same transaction.
   188→
   189→    The caller is responsible for committing the session.
   190→
   191→    Args:
   192→        session: Active SQLModel session (not yet committed).
   193→        entity: The SQLModel entity to persist.
   194→        event_type: Domain event kind.
   195→        aggregate_type: Type of aggregate (e.g. "protocol").
   196→        aggregate_id: ID of the affected aggregate.
   197→        payload: Event payload dict.
   198→        idempotency_key: Dedup key; auto-generated if omitted.
   199→
   200→    Returns:
   201→        The created OutboxEvent (uncommitted).
   202→    """
   203→    ikey = idempotency_key or (f"{event_type.value}:{aggregate_id}:{uuid4()}")
   204→
   205→    outbox_event = OutboxEvent(
   206→        event_type=event_type.value,
   207→        aggregate_type=aggregate_type,
   208→        aggregate_id=aggregate_id,
   209→        payload=payload,
   210→        idempotency_key=ikey,
   211→        status="pending",
   212→    )
   213→
   214→    session.add(entity)
   215→    session.add(outbox_event)
   216→
   217→    return outbox_event
   218→
```

> AGENT

The fix is to give the outbox processor its own dedicated thread pool executor so it doesn't compete with FastAPI's sync endpoint threads:

> TOOL

  "old_string": "from __future__ import annotations\n\nimport asyncio\nimport logging\nfrom datetime import datetime, timezone\nfrom typing import Any, Callable\nfrom uuid import uuid4\n\nfrom shared.models import OutboxEvent\nfrom sqlalchemy import Engine\nfrom sqlmodel import Session, col, select\n\nfrom events_py.models import DomainEventKind\n\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n\nMAX_RETRIES = 3\n\n\nclass OutboxProcessor:\n    \"\"\"Processes pending outbox events with at-least-once delivery.\n\n    Usage:\n        processor = OutboxProcessor(engine, handlers={\n            \"protocol_uploaded\": [handle_protocol_uploaded],\n        })\n        await processor.start()  # Runs polling loop\n        await processor.stop()   # Graceful shutdown\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        engine: Engine,\n        handlers: dict[str, list[Callable[..., Any]]] | None = None,\n        poll_interval: float = 1.0,\n        batch_size: int = 100,\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Initialize the outbox processor.\n\n        Args:\n            engine: SQLAlchemy database engine.\n            handlers: Maps event_type string to handler callbacks.\n            poll_interval: Seconds between polls.\n            batch_size: Max events per poll cycle.\n        \"\"\"\n        self.engine = engine\n        self.handlers: dict[str, list[Callable[..., Any]]] = handlers or {}\n        self.poll_interval = poll_interval\n        self.batch_size = batch_size\n        self._shutdown_event = asyncio.Event()",
  "new_string": "from __future__ import annotations\n\nimport asyncio\nimport logging\nfrom concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor\nfrom datetime import datetime, timezone\nfrom typing import Any, Callable\nfrom uuid import uuid4\n\nfrom shared.models import OutboxEvent\nfrom sqlalchemy import Engine\nfrom sqlmodel import Session, col, select\n\nfrom events_py.models import DomainEventKind\n\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n\nMAX_RETRIES = 3\n\n\nclass OutboxProcessor:\n    \"\"\"Processes pending outbox events with at-least-once delivery.\n\n    Usage:\n        processor = OutboxProcessor(engine, handlers={\n            \"protocol_uploaded\": [handle_protocol_uploaded],\n        })\n        await processor.start()  # Runs polling loop\n        await processor.stop()   # Graceful shutdown\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        engine: Engine,\n        handlers: dict[str, list[Callable[..., Any]]] | None = None,\n        poll_interval: float = 1.0,\n        batch_size: int = 100,\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Initialize the outbox processor.\n\n        Args:\n            engine: SQLAlchemy database engine.\n            handlers: Maps event_type string to handler callbacks.\n            poll_interval: Seconds between polls.\n            batch_size: Max events per poll cycle.\n        \"\"\"\n        self.engine = engine\n        self.handlers: dict[str, list[Callable[..., Any]]] = handlers or {}\n        self.poll_interval = poll_interval\n        self.batch_size = batch_size\n        self._shutdown_event = asyncio.Event()\n        # Dedicated thread pool so pipeline handlers don't starve\n        # FastAPI's default executor (used for sync endpoints).\n        self._executor = ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=2, thread_name_prefix=\"outbox\")"
}

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/libs/events-py/src/events_py/outbox.py",
  "old_string": "    async def start(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Run the polling loop until stop() is called.\"\"\"\n        logger.info(\n            \"Outbox processor started (interval=%.1fs, batch_size=%d)\",\n            self.poll_interval,\n            self.batch_size,\n        )\n        while not self._shutdown_event.is_set():\n            try:\n                count = await asyncio.get_event_loop().run_in_executor(\n                    None, self.poll_and_process\n                )\n                if count > 0:\n                    logger.info(\"Processed %d outbox events\", count)\n            except Exception:\n                logger.exception(\"Error in outbox processor poll cycle\")\n            try:\n                await asyncio.wait_for(\n                    self._shutdown_event.wait(),\n                    timeout=self.poll_interval,\n                )\n            except asyncio.TimeoutError:\n                pass\n        logger.info(\"Outbox processor stopped\")\n\n    async def stop(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Signal the processor to stop after current poll.\"\"\"\n        logger.info(\"Outbox processor shutdown requested\")\n        self._shutdown_event.set()",
  "new_string": "    async def start(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Run the polling loop until stop() is called.\"\"\"\n        logger.info(\n            \"Outbox processor started (interval=%.1fs, batch_size=%d)\",\n            self.poll_interval,\n            self.batch_size,\n        )\n        loop = asyncio.get_event_loop()\n        while not self._shutdown_event.is_set():\n            try:\n                count = await loop.run_in_executor(\n                    self._executor, self.poll_and_process\n                )\n                if count > 0:\n                    logger.info(\"Processed %d outbox events\", count)\n            except Exception:\n                logger.exception(\"Error in outbox processor poll cycle\")\n            try:\n                await asyncio.wait_for(\n                    self._shutdown_event.wait(),\n                    timeout=self.poll_interval,\n                )\n            except asyncio.TimeoutError:\n                pass\n        logger.info(\"Outbox processor stopped\")\n\n    async def stop(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Signal the processor to stop after current poll.\"\"\"\n        logger.info(\"Outbox processor shutdown requested\")\n        self._shutdown_event.set()\n        self._executor.shutdown(wait=False)"
}

All 11 tests pass across both packages. Here's the full diagnosis:

## Root Cause Analysis

**Two bugs were found, both contributing to the "stuck uploading" behavior:**

### Bug 1: Premature event firing (original error)
`POST /protocols/upload` published the `ProtocolUploaded` outbox event **before** the client PUT the actual file bytes. The outbox processor picked it up and the pipeline failed with `FileNotFoundError`.

**Fix:** Moved `persist_with_outbox` from `upload_protocol` to `confirm_upload` (`protocols.py`), so the event only fires after the file is confirmed present.

### Bug 2: Thread pool starvation (the hang)
The outbox processor used `run_in_executor(None, ...)` — the **default** thread pool executor. FastAPI also uses this same default executor for all sync endpoint handlers. When the pipeline handler ran (which blocks a thread for the entire LLM pipeline duration), it starved the thread pool, making **all** API endpoints unresponsive — including `/health`.

This is what you saw: 5 browser connections ESTABLISHED but never completing, curl timing out on `/health`.

**Fix:** Gave `OutboxProcessor` its own dedicated `ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=2)` (`outbox.py`), isolating pipeline work from API request handling.

### Files changed:
- `services/api-service/src/api_service/protocols.py` — move event to confirm-upload
- `libs/events-py/src/events_py/outbox.py` — dedicated thread pool for outbox processing

Both fixes verified. Here's the summary:

## Two bugs found and fixed

### Bug 1: Race condition — event fires before file exists
**File:** `services/api-service/src/api_service/protocols.py`

**Root cause:** `POST /protocols/upload` published the `ProtocolUploaded` outbox event immediately, but the PDF file isn't uploaded until the browser PUTs it in step 2. The outbox processor picks up the event before the file exists.

**Fix:** Moved the `persist_with_outbox` call from `upload_protocol` to `confirm_upload`, which is step 3 — called only after the file is confirmed uploaded.

### Bug 2: Thread pool starvation — pipeline blocks all API endpoints
**File:** `libs/events-py/src/events_py/outbox.py`

**Root cause:** The outbox processor used `run_in_executor(None, ...)` — the **default** thread pool, which is shared with FastAPI's sync endpoint handlers. The pipeline handler (`handle_protocol_uploaded`) runs the entire LLM pipeline synchronously via `asyncio.run()`, occupying a thread for minutes. This starves FastAPI — no sync endpoints (including `/health`) can respond.

**Fix:** Gave the outbox processor a dedicated `ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=2)` so pipeline execution never blocks API request handling.

## 1. MLflow ContextVar warnings
**Root cause:** Already documented as Issue #8 in your codebase. `mlflow.langchain.autolog()` is called in both `main.py` (startup, main thread) and `trigger.py:217` (inside `asyncio.run()` in a thread pool worker). MLflow's autologging uses `ContextVar` tokens, but the outbox processor runs handlers in a dedicated thread pool (our fix). The ContextVar created in the main thread's context doesn't propagate to the worker thread, so MLflow warns every time it tries to access the autolog context across the boundary.

**Impact:** Cosmetic — tracing still works, just some spans may not link to parents.

**Fix:** Suppress the specific MLflow autologging warning via a logging filter, since it's a known MLflow limitation with threaded execution that you can't fix upstream.

## 2. Google GenAI aiohttp deprecation
```

```
**Root cause:** Internal to the `google-genai` SDK (`_api_client.py:755`). Their `AiohttpClientSession` subclasses `aiohttp.ClientSession`, which `aiohttp` now discourages.

**Impact:** Zero — this is a warning inside Google's SDK code, not yours. Will be fixed in a future SDK release.

**Fix:** Suppress with a `warnings.filterwarnings` for this specific deprecation.

## 3. AFC info message
```
INFO:google_genai.models:AFC is enabled with max remote calls: 10.
```
**Root cause:** Informational log from the Google GenAI SDK about Automatic Function Calling being enabled.

**Impact:** None — purely informational.

**Fix:** Set the `google_genai.models` logger to WARNING level.

## google-genai Package Research Results

### Latest Version

The latest version is **1.63.0**, released on February 11, 2026. You are currently on 1.62.0, so there is one patch release available.

### aiohttp ClientSession Inheritance Deprecation Warning — NOT FIXED

The warning is **not fixed** in any released version including 1.63.0. Here is the full status:

- **Issue #1989** ("DeprecationWarning: Inheritance class AiohttpClientSession from ClientSession is discouraged") was filed on January 26, 2026 and is currently **open with no fix timeline**.
- The root cause is in `_api_client.py` — the SDK's internal `AiohttpClientSession` class directly inherits from `aiohttp.ClientSession`, which aiohttp has discouraged since ~2018 and now emits a `DeprecationWarning` for.
- As of February 5, 2026, a maintainer acknowledged the bug and stated "We will file a bug internally." No PR or fix has been merged.
- The warning becomes a hard error if your test suite or runtime uses `warnings.filterwarnings("error")`.

The only mitigation currently available (added in v1.58.0) is to supply your own managed session via `HttpOptions.aiohttp_client`, bypassing the SDK's internal subclassed session entirely.

- Fixed `base_url` and global location parsing
- Removed build warning from extra comma in assertion
- Removed debug print statement
- Removed unused `websockets` import

**Breaking Changes:** None documented in 1.63.0.

Here is a detailed summary of what research found:

---

## The Root Cause

The warning:
```
WARNING mlflow.utils.autologging_utils: Encountered unexpected error during autologging: <Token var=<ContextVar name='current_context' ...> was created in a different Context
```

is a Python-level `ValueError` raised by the `ContextVar.reset(token)` mechanism. When `asyncio.run()` is called inside a `ThreadPoolExecutor` worker, it creates a brand-new event loop in a new execution context. Any `ContextVar` tokens that MLflow's tracing system set in the parent thread's context cannot be reset from within that new context — Python raises `ValueError` when you call `token.reset()` on a token that was created in a different `contextvars.Context` object. MLflow's autologging catches broad exceptions and surfaces this as a WARNING rather than a crash.

---

## 1. Has a Newer MLflow Version Fixed This?

**Not directly, and not for your exact pattern.** Here is the version timeline of related fixes:

| Version | Date | PR | What It Does |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2.17.3 | Nov 2024 | [#13672](https://github.com/mlflow/mlflow/pull/13672) | Fixes LangChain thread-safety by using thread-local disabling instead of global, prevents duplicate traces in multi-thread agent eval |
| 3.5.0 | Oct 2025 | [#17219](https://github.com/mlflow/mlflow/pull/17219) | `mlflow.tracing.set_destination` now uses `ContextVar` instead of `ThreadLocalVariable` — for async isolation of destination config, not for the autolog token issue |
| 3.5.0 | Oct 2025 | [#17273](https://github.com/mlflow/mlflow/pull/17273) | Fixes parent span resolution for LangChain, flattened async trace hierarchy |
| 3.8.0 | Dec 2025 | [#19228](https://github.com/mlflow/mlflow/pull/19228) | Adds `run_tracer_inline` parameter — fixes context propagation when using `@mlflow.trace` decorators inside LangGraph async nodes with `ainvoke` |
| 3.9.0 | Jan 2026 | [#19797](https://github.com/mlflow/mlflow/pull/19797) | Isolates MLflow's `RuntimeContext` from OpenTelemetry's global one — fixes entangled traces when both SDKs are active together |

**None of these specifically address the pattern of `asyncio.run()` called from a `ThreadPoolExecutor` worker.** That pattern creates a fundamentally new execution context where token reset semantics break. As of MLflow 3.9.0 (the latest as of February 2026), this specific case is **not fixed** at the library level. Issue [#15131](https://github.com/mlflow/mlflow/issues/15131) — which requests replacing `ThreadLocalVariable` with `ContextVar` in the core fluent tracking API (`mlflow.tracking.fluent`) — remains open as a feature request.

---

## 2. The Right Pattern: `contextvars.copy_context()` with ThreadPoolExecutor

The **official MLflow-documented workaround** for threading is to propagate context manually using `contextvars.copy_context()`. From the [Tracing FAQ](https://mlflow.org/docs/latest/genai/tracing/faq/):

```python
import contextvars
import mlflow
from concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor

@mlflow.trace
def worker_function(data):
    # MLflow trace context is available here because ctx.run() was used
    return process_data(data)

@mlflow.trace
def main_function(data_list):
    with ThreadPoolExecutor() as executor:
        futures = []
        for data in data_list:
            # Snapshot the current ContextVar state before submitting
            ctx = contextvars.copy_context()
            # ctx.run() executes the function inside the copied context
            futures.append(executor.submit(ctx.run, worker_function, data))
        results = [future.result() for future in futures]
    return results
```

**Why this matters for your case:** When you call `asyncio.run()` inside a `ThreadPoolExecutor` worker, `asyncio.run()` creates a fresh `Context` internally. If you pre-copy the context using `ctx.run()` as the wrapper, the worker thread starts with MLflow's ContextVars already set to the correct values, and any `reset()` calls operate on tokens created in that same copied context. The mismatch disappears.

The pattern to apply in your `ThreadPoolExecutor` scenario:

```python
import asyncio
import contextvars
from concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor

import mlflow

mlflow.langchain.autolog()

def thread_worker(graph, inputs):
    # The ctx.run() wrapper (applied by the executor) has already set up
    # the correct ContextVar state. asyncio.run() here creates a new event
    # loop but the MLflow ContextVars are already in scope.
    return asyncio.run(graph.ainvoke(inputs))

def run_parallel(graph, inputs_list):
    with ThreadPoolExecutor() as executor:
        futures = []
        for inputs in inputs_list:
            ctx = contextvars.copy_context()
            futures.append(executor.submit(ctx.run, thread_worker, graph, inputs))
        return [f.result() for f in futures]
```

---

## 3. MLflow Autolog Parameters That Help

**`run_tracer_inline=True`** (added in MLflow 3.8.0 via [#19228](https://github.com/mlflow/mlflow/pull/19228)):

```python
mlflow.langchain.autolog(run_tracer_inline=True)
```

This makes the `MlflowLangchainTracer` callback run in the main async task rather than being offloaded to a thread pool. This is specifically designed for the case where you use manual `@mlflow.trace` decorators inside LangGraph nodes alongside `autolog`, but it also reduces the number of async/thread context crossings the tracer makes internally. The official caveat from the docs: avoid calling multiple graph invocations sequentially within the same async function with this enabled, as it can cause trace merging.

**`silent=True`** — suppresses the warning without fixing anything:

```python
mlflow.langchain.autolog(silent=True)
```

This is a blunt instrument. It will silence the `WARNING mlflow.utils.autologging_utils: Encountered unexpected error...` message entirely, but the underlying ContextVar token reset failure still happens, just quietly. Traces may be incomplete or malformed.

**`log_traces=False`** — disables tracing entirely to avoid the problem:

```python
mlflow.langchain.autolog(log_traces=False)
```

This bypasses the tracing machinery that creates ContextVar tokens in the first place, eliminating the error at the cost of losing all LangChain/LangGraph trace data.

---

## 4. The OpenTelemetry Context Isolation Fix in 3.9.0

The [#19797](https://github.com/mlflow/mlflow/pull/19797) fix in 3.9.0 is worth noting. It addresses a related but different problem: when both MLflow tracing and OpenTelemetry auto-instrumentation (e.g., from FastAPI or Celery) are active simultaneously, they shared a global `RuntimeContext` instance, causing entangled parent-child spans and the "ContextVar created in a different Context" error during context detach operations. The fix gives MLflow its own isolated `RuntimeContext`.

If you have any OTel auto-instrumentation active in your environment alongside `mlflow.langchain.autolog()`, upgrading to 3.9.0 would specifically address this class of errors. The environment variable `MLFLOW_USE_DEFAULT_TRACER_PROVIDER=false` was a pre-fix workaround.

---

## Summary of Recommendations

1. **Upgrade to MLflow 3.9.0** (you are already on 3.9.0 per the question — you are on the latest release). No further version upgrade is currently available.

2. **Use `contextvars.copy_context()` + `ctx.run()`** as the wrapper when submitting work to `ThreadPoolExecutor`. This is the official documented pattern and directly prevents the token mismatch.

3. **Set `run_tracer_inline=True`** in `mlflow.langchain.autolog()` — this reduces internal thread-pool offloading within the tracer itself.

4. **Check for OTel instrumentation conflicts** — if you have `opentelemetry-instrumentation-*` packages active, 3.9.0's `#19797` fix already handles context isolation, but verify `MLFLOW_USE_DEFAULT_TRACER_PROVIDER` is not set to `true` if you want MLflow and OTel spans to be unified.

5. **If warnings are acceptable but need to be suppressed**, use `silent=True` as a last resort while tracking [issue #15131](https://github.com/mlflow/mlflow/issues/15131) for a proper upstream fix to MLflow's fluent API context management.

---

Here is a complete guide covering all five areas you asked about, based on the MLflow 3.9.0 documentation, release notes, source code, and GitHub examples.

---

## Important Architecture Distinction for MLflow 3.9.0

MLflow 3.9.0 made a major architectural shift: **the AI Gateway is now embedded directly inside the tracking server process**. You no longer run a separate `mlflow gateway start` process alongside `mlflow server`. The two are unified. However, the **legacy standalone gateway** (`mlflow gateway start`) still works and is documented. The new integrated gateway is configured through the UI or API rather than just a static YAML file.

There are two distinct approaches. This guide covers both.

---

## 1. Config File Format for Defining the Claude Endpoint

### Approach A: New Integrated Gateway (MLflow 3.x recommended)

In MLflow 3.x, the gateway is managed via the UI at `http://localhost:5000/#/gateway`. You create endpoints through the web interface (no YAML file required). The UI will show you available Anthropic models with context window and pricing metadata.

If you prefer file-based configuration (the legacy/deployments path), use this YAML structure:

```yaml
# gateway-config.yaml
endpoints:
  - name: claude-chat
    endpoint_type: llm/v1/chat
    model:
      provider: anthropic
      name: claude-3-5-sonnet-20241022
      config:
        anthropic_api_key: $ANTHROPIC_API_KEY

  - name: claude-completions
    endpoint_type: llm/v1/completions
    model:
      provider: anthropic
      name: claude-3-opus-20240229
      config:
        anthropic_api_key: $ANTHROPIC_API_KEY
```

**Anthropic-specific fields from `AnthropicConfig` (source: [mlflow.gateway.config](https://www.mlflow.org/docs/3.3.2/api_reference/_modules/mlflow/gateway/config.html)):**

| Field | Required | Default | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `anthropic_api_key` | Yes | — | Resolves from env var, file path, or literal string |
| `anthropic_version` | No | `2023-06-01` | Anthropic API version header |

**Supported endpoint types for Anthropic:**
- `llm/v1/chat` - conversational (chat messages format)
- `llm/v1/completions` - text completion
- `llm/v1/embeddings` - **NOT supported** by Anthropic in MLflow gateway

**Current Claude model names to use in `name:`:**
- `claude-3-5-sonnet-20241022`
- `claude-3-5-haiku-20241022`
- `claude-3-opus-20240229`
- `claude-3-sonnet-20240229`
- `claude-3-haiku-20240307`

### Approach B: Passthrough Endpoint (MLflow 3.9+ new feature)

MLflow 3.9.0 added **native passthrough support for the Anthropic Messages API**, meaning you can forward raw Anthropic API calls without translation:

```yaml
endpoints:
  - name: anthropic-passthrough
    endpoint_type: llm/v1/chat
    model:
      provider: anthropic
      name: claude-3-5-sonnet-20241022
      config:
        anthropic_api_key: $ANTHROPIC_API_KEY
    # Passthrough routes the native Anthropic Messages API format directly
```

This is particularly useful for accessing Anthropic-specific features (extended thinking, vision, tool use in native format) that MLflow's normalized API might not expose.

### Endpoint Naming Rules

The `name` field only allows alphanumeric characters, hyphens, and underscores — no spaces. It becomes part of the API URL path.

### Optional: Rate Limiting

```yaml
endpoints:
  - name: claude-chat
    endpoint_type: llm/v1/chat
    model:
      provider: anthropic
      name: claude-3-5-sonnet-20241022
      config:
        anthropic_api_key: $ANTHROPIC_API_KEY
    limit:
      calls: 10
      renewal_period: 60   # seconds
```

---

## 2. Environment Variables

### Required

```bash
ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=sk-ant-api03-...
```

This is the only required env var. The config YAML references it as `$ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`.

### Gateway-Related Environment Variables (from [mlflow.environment_variables](https://mlflow.org/docs/latest/python_api/mlflow.environment_variables.html))

| Variable | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `MLFLOW_GATEWAY_CONFIG` | Path to the config file for MLflow AI Gateway (legacy gateway) |
| `MLFLOW_DEPLOYMENTS_CONFIG` | Configuration file path for AI Gateway (deployments server path) |
| `MLFLOW_GATEWAY_URI` | URI of a running Gateway server for client APIs |
| `MLFLOW_DEPLOYMENTS_TARGET` | URI for MLflow AI Gateway instance (client-side) |

For the **integrated 3.x gateway** embedded in `mlflow server`, you pass the config path via a CLI flag (see section 5), and `MLFLOW_GATEWAY_CONFIG` can also serve as the default.

---

## 3. The MLflow Assistant Beta Feature

**Critical clarification**: The MLflow Assistant in 3.9.0 is **not** a self-hosted LLM endpoint you configure. It is a **Claude Code extension/plugin** — meaning it requires the Claude Code CLI application to be installed on the developer's local machine. It is not a standalone chatbot backed by a self-hosted model.

How it works:
- There is a floating "Assistant" button in the bottom-right corner of the MLflow UI.
- Clicking it opens a chat panel that passes your current UI context (which traces, runs, evaluations you are viewing) directly to Claude Code running locally.
- It uses your existing Claude Code subscription — **no additional Anthropic API key is consumed by MLflow itself** for this feature.
- It is "backed by Claude Code" in the sense that it sends context from the MLflow UI to the local Claude Code process via MCP (Model Context Protocol).

**To enable it:**
1. Install Claude Code CLI locally.
2. Open the MLflow UI at `http://localhost:5000`.
3. Click the floating "Assistant" button in the bottom-right corner.
4. Follow the setup wizard to connect Claude Code to the MLflow instance.

**This means**: if you want self-hosted LLM inference (e.g., your own Anthropic API key routing through MLflow), that is the AI Gateway feature, not the Assistant. The Assistant is a developer-tooling integration, not a self-hosted chatbot backend.

---

## 4. Running the Gateway Server (Legacy Standalone Approach)

If you want to run the standalone gateway process (still valid in 3.9.0, labeled "legacy" in docs):

```bash
# Start the server
mlflow gateway start \
  --config-path /path/to/gateway-config.yaml \
  --host 0.0.0.0 \
  --port 5001 \
  --workers 2
```

Or via environment variable:

```bash
export REDACTED.yaml
mlflow gateway start --host 0.0.0.0 --port 5001
```

The gateway supports **hot-reloading**: edit `gateway-config.yaml` and changes are detected automatically without restarting the server.

Verify the server:
```bash
curl http://localhost:5001/health
# Interactive docs available at:
# http://localhost:5001/docs
```

---

## 5. Docker-Specific Setup for `ghcr.io/mlflow/mlflow:v3.9.0`

### Option A: New Integrated Gateway (recommended for 3.9.0)

The tracking server and gateway run as a single process. Pass the gateway config path via an environment variable or a flag when starting `mlflow server`. The key is to **mount your config file** into the container.

```yaml
# docker-compose.yml
version: "3.8"

services:
  mlflow:
    image: ghcr.io/mlflow/mlflow:v3.9.0
    ports:
      - "5000:5000"
    environment:
      - ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=${ANTHROPIC_API_KEY}
      - REDACTED.yaml
    volumes:
      - ./gateway-config.yaml:/mlflow/gateway-config.yaml:ro
      - mlflow-data:/mlflow/mlruns
    command: >
      mlflow server
        --host 0.0.0.0
        --port 5000
        --backend-store-uri sqlite:////mlflow/mlruns/mlflow.db
        --default-artifact-root /mlflow/mlruns

volumes:
  mlflow-data:
```

Your `.env` file (never commit this):

```bash
# .env
REDACTED
```

Run with:
```bash
docker compose --env-file .env up -d
```

### Option B: Standalone Gateway Server in Docker (Legacy approach)

Run the gateway as a separate container from your tracking server:

```yaml
# docker-compose.yml
version: "3.8"

services:
  mlflow-tracking:
    image: ghcr.io/mlflow/mlflow:v3.9.0
    ports:
      - "5000:5000"
    volumes:
      - mlflow-data:/mlflow/mlruns
    command: >
      mlflow server
        --host 0.0.0.0
        --port 5000
        --backend-store-uri sqlite:////mlflow/mlruns/mlflow.db
        --default-artifact-root /mlflow/mlruns

  mlflow-gateway:
    image: ghcr.io/mlflow/mlflow:v3.9.0
    ports:
      - "5001:5001"
    environment:
      - ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=${ANTHROPIC_API_KEY}
    volumes:
      - ./gateway-config.yaml:/mlflow/gateway-config.yaml:ro
    command: >
      mlflow gateway start
        --config-path /mlflow/gateway-config.yaml
        --host 0.0.0.0
        --port 5001
        --workers 2

volumes:
  mlflow-data:
```

### The gateway-config.yaml to place alongside docker-compose.yml

```yaml
# gateway-config.yaml
endpoints:
  - name: claude-chat
    endpoint_type: llm/v1/chat
    model:
      provider: anthropic
      name: claude-3-5-sonnet-20241022
      config:
        anthropic_api_key: $ANTHROPIC_API_KEY

  - name: claude-haiku-chat
    endpoint_type: llm/v1/chat
    model:
      provider: anthropic
      name: claude-3-5-haiku-20241022
      config:
        anthropic_api_key: $ANTHROPIC_API_KEY

  - name: claude-completions
    endpoint_type: llm/v1/completions
    model:
      provider: anthropic
      name: claude-3-opus-20240229
      config:
        anthropic_api_key: $ANTHROPIC_API_KEY
```

### Key Docker Considerations

1. **Never hardcode the API key** in your YAML or Dockerfile. Always use `$ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` in the YAML and pass the actual value through Docker's `-e` flag or an `.env` file.

2. **The `--host 0.0.0.0` flag is essential** inside Docker. The default `127.0.0.1` bind address will make the server unreachable from outside the container.

3. **Volume mount the config file as read-only** (`:ro` suffix). The gateway's hot-reload watches for file changes, so if you later edit the file on the host, changes propagate to the container automatically.

4. **Port mapping**: The gateway and tracking server should be on separate ports if running together (e.g., tracking on 5000, gateway on 5001).

5. **The `mlflow[genai]` extras** are included in the official Docker image, so you do not need a custom Dockerfile for basic gateway functionality.

6. **Windows is not supported** for the gateway server. This is not a concern if you are running the Docker image on Linux or macOS.

---

## Querying the Gateway Endpoint (Verification)

Once running, test your Claude endpoint:

```bash
# Chat endpoint
curl -X POST http://localhost:5001/gateway/claude-chat/invocations \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d '{
    "messages": [{"role": "user", "content": "Hello, Claude!"}],
    "temperature": 0.7,
    "max_tokens": 256
  }'

# Or with the MLflow Python client
import mlflow.deployments
client = mlflow.deployments.get_deploy_client("http://localhost:5001")
response = client.predict(
    endpoint="claude-chat",
    inputs={"messages": [{"role": "user", "content": "Summarize MLflow in one sentence."}]}
)
print(response)
```

---

## Summary of What Each Feature Actually Is

| Feature | What it requires | Anthropic API Key needed? |
|---|---|---|
| AI Gateway endpoints | `gateway-config.yaml` + ANTHROPIC_API_KEY env var in the server process | Yes, server-side |
| MLflow Assistant (UI button) | Claude Code CLI installed locally on the developer machine | Uses Claude Code subscription, not the gateway |
| Passthrough Anthropic API | Same as AI Gateway config | Yes, server-side |
| LiteLLM provider (new in 3.9) | LiteLLM config in gateway YAML | Depends on provider |

The MLflow Assistant beta is a **local developer tool** tied to Claude Code, not a self-hosted model endpoint. The AI Gateway is the right feature if you want centralized, server-side routing of Anthropic API calls.

     1→"""Outbox processor for publishing pending domain events.
     2→
     3→Implements the transactional outbox pattern with at-least-once delivery.
     4→The OutboxProcessor polls the outbox table for pending events and dispatches
     5→them to registered handlers. The persist_with_outbox helper enables atomic
     6→entity + event writes in a single database transaction.
     7→"""
     8→
     9→from __future__ import annotations
    10→
    11→import asyncio
    12→import logging
    13→from concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor
    14→from datetime import datetime, timezone
    15→from typing import Any, Callable
    16→from uuid import uuid4
    17→
    18→from shared.models import OutboxEvent
    19→from sqlalchemy import Engine
    20→from sqlmodel import Session, col, select
    21→
    22→from events_py.models import DomainEventKind
    23→
    24→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    25→
    26→MAX_RETRIES = 3
    27→
    28→
    29→class OutboxProcessor:
    30→    """Processes pending outbox events with at-least-once delivery.
    31→
    32→    Usage:
    33→        processor = OutboxProcessor(engine, handlers={
    34→            "protocol_uploaded": [handle_protocol_uploaded],
    35→        })
    36→        await processor.start()  # Runs polling loop
    37→        await processor.stop()   # Graceful shutdown
    38→    """
    39→
    40→    def __init__(
    41→        self,
    42→        engine: Engine,
    43→        handlers: dict[str, list[Callable[..., Any]]] | None = None,
    44→        poll_interval: float = 1.0,
    45→        batch_size: int = 100,
    46→    ) -> None:
    47→        """Initialize the outbox processor.
    48→
    49→        Args:
    50→            engine: SQLAlchemy database engine.
    51→            handlers: Maps event_type string to handler callbacks.
    52→            poll_interval: Seconds between polls.
    53→            batch_size: Max events per poll cycle.
    54→        """
    55→        self.engine = engine
    56→        self.handlers: dict[str, list[Callable[..., Any]]] = handlers or {}
    57→        self.poll_interval = poll_interval
    58→        self.batch_size = batch_size
    59→        self._shutdown_event = asyncio.Event()
    60→        # Dedicated thread pool so pipeline handlers don't starve
    61→        # FastAPI's default executor (used for sync endpoints).
    62→        self._executor = ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=2, thread_name_prefix="outbox")
    63→
    64→    def poll_and_process(self) -> int:
    65→        """Poll for pending events and process them.
    66→
    67→        Returns:
    68→            Number of events processed in this poll cycle.
    69→        """
    70→        processed = 0
    71→        with Session(self.engine) as session:
    72→            statement = (
    73→                select(OutboxEvent)
    74→                .where(col(OutboxEvent.status).in_(["pending", "failed"]))
    75→                .order_by(OutboxEvent.created_at.asc())  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
    76→                .limit(self.batch_size)
    77→            )
    78→
    79→            # Use FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED on PostgreSQL
    80→            # for concurrent processor safety
    81→            db_url = str(self.engine.url)
    82→            if "postgresql" in db_url:
    83→                statement = statement.with_for_update(skip_locked=True)
    84→
    85→            events = session.exec(statement).all()
    86→
    87→            for event in events:
    88→                event_handlers = self.handlers.get(event.event_type, [])
    89→
    90→                if not event_handlers:
    91→                    logger.debug(
    92→                        "No handlers for event type: %s",
    93→                        event.event_type,
    94→                    )
    95→
    96→                try:
    97→                    for handler in event_handlers:
    98→                        handler(event.payload)
    99→
   100→                    event.status = "published"
   101→                    event.published_at = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
   102→                    session.add(event)
   103→                    processed += 1
   104→
   105→                    logger.info(
   106→                        "Published event %s (type=%s)",
   107→                        event.id,
   108→                        event.event_type,
   109→                    )
   110→                except Exception:
   111→                    event.retry_count += 1
   112→                    if event.retry_count >= MAX_RETRIES:
   113→                        event.status = "dead_letter"
   114→                        logger.warning(
   115→                            "Event %s exhausted retries (%d), marking as dead_letter",
   116→                            event.id,
   117→                            event.retry_count,
   118→                        )
   119→                        # Update protocol status to dead_letter if protocol event
   120→                        if event.aggregate_type == "protocol":
   121→                            from shared.models import Protocol
   122→
   123→                            protocol = session.get(Protocol, event.aggregate_id)
   124→                            if protocol:
   125→                                protocol.status = "dead_letter"
   126→                                protocol.error_reason = "Maximum retries exceeded"
   127→                                protocol.metadata_ = {
   128→                                    **protocol.metadata_,
   129→                                    "error": {
   130→                                        "category": "pipeline_failed",
   131→                                        "reason": "Maximum retries exceeded",
   132→                                        "retry_count": event.retry_count,
   133→                                    },
   134→                                }
   135→                                session.add(protocol)
   136→                    else:
   137→                        event.status = "failed"
   138→
   139→                    session.add(event)
   140→                    logger.exception(
   141→                        "Failed to process event %s (type=%s, retry_count=%d)",
   142→                        event.id,
   143→                        event.event_type,
   144→                        event.retry_count,
   145→                    )
   146→
   147→            session.commit()
   148→
   149→        return processed
   150→
   151→    async def start(self) -> None:
   152→        """Run the polling loop until stop() is called."""
   153→        logger.info(
   154→            "Outbox processor started (interval=%.1fs, batch_size=%d)",
   155→            self.poll_interval,
   156→            self.batch_size,
   157→        )
   158→        loop = asyncio.get_event_loop()
   159→        while not self._shutdown_event.is_set():
   160→            try:
   161→                count = await loop.run_in_executor(
   162→                    self._executor, self.poll_and_process
   163→                )
   164→                if count > 0:
   165→                    logger.info("Processed %d outbox events", count)
   166→            except Exception:
   167→                logger.exception("Error in outbox processor poll cycle")
   168→            try:
   169→                await asyncio.wait_for(
   170→                    self._shutdown_event.wait(),
   171→                    timeout=self.poll_interval,
   172→                )
   173→            except asyncio.TimeoutError:
   174→                pass
   175→        logger.info("Outbox processor stopped")
   176→
   177→    async def stop(self) -> None:
   178→        """Signal the processor to stop after current poll."""
   179→        logger.info("Outbox processor shutdown requested")
   180→        self._shutdown_event.set()
   181→        self._executor.shutdown(wait=False)
   182→
   183→
   184→def persist_with_outbox(
   185→    session: Session,
   186→    entity: Any,
   187→    event_type: DomainEventKind,
   188→    aggregate_type: str,
   189→    aggregate_id: str,
   190→    payload: dict[str, Any],
   191→    idempotency_key: str | None = None,
   192→) -> OutboxEvent:
   193→    """Persist an entity and its outbox event in the same transaction.
   194→
   195→    The caller is responsible for committing the session.
   196→
   197→    Args:
   198→        session: Active SQLModel session (not yet committed).
   199→        entity: The SQLModel entity to persist.
   200→        event_type: Domain event kind.
   201→        aggregate_type: Type of aggregate (e.g. "protocol").
   202→        aggregate_id: ID of the affected aggregate.
   203→        payload: Event payload dict.
   204→        idempotency_key: Dedup key; auto-generated if omitted.
   205→
   206→    Returns:
   207→        The created OutboxEvent (uncommitted).
   208→    """
   209→    ikey = idempotency_key or (f"{event_type.value}:{aggregate_id}:{uuid4()}")
   210→
   211→    outbox_event = OutboxEvent(
   212→        event_type=event_type.value,
   213→        aggregate_type=aggregate_type,
   214→        aggregate_id=aggregate_id,
   215→        payload=payload,
   216→        idempotency_key=ikey,
   217→        status="pending",
   218→    )
   219→
   220→    session.add(entity)
   221→    session.add(outbox_event)
   222→
   223→    return outbox_event
   224→
```

Now fix the MLflow ContextVar issue. The fix has two parts:

1. **Outbox processor**: use `contextvars.copy_context()` when submitting to the thread pool
2. **Trigger**: add `run_tracer_inline=True` to `mlflow.langchain.autolog()`

  "new_string": "import asyncio\nimport contextvars\nimport logging\nfrom concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor\nfrom datetime import datetime, timezone\nfrom typing import Any, Callable\nfrom uuid import uuid4"
}

Now update the `start` method to propagate the context:

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01676CDrczmkrZr7P5Q2CStP
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/libs/events-py/src/events_py/outbox.py",
  "old_string": "        loop = asyncio.get_event_loop()\n        while not self._shutdown_event.is_set():\n            try:\n                count = await loop.run_in_executor(\n                    self._executor, self.poll_and_process\n                )",
  "new_string": "        loop = asyncio.get_event_loop()\n        while not self._shutdown_event.is_set():\n            try:\n                # Copy context so MLflow ContextVars propagate to the\n                # worker thread, avoiding \"created in a different Context\"\n                # warnings when autologging resets tokens.\n                ctx = contextvars.copy_context()\n                count = await loop.run_in_executor(\n                    self._executor, ctx.run, self.poll_and_process\n                )"
}

> AGENT

All three tasks done. Here's the summary:

## Changes Made

### 1. Updated google-genai to 1.63.0
- `pyproject.toml` — bumped version floor from `>=1.55.0` to `>=1.63.0`
- `services/protocol-processor-service/pyproject.toml` — same
- `uv.lock` — resolved to 1.63.0

### 2. Fixed MLflow ContextVar warnings
- **`libs/events-py/src/events_py/outbox.py`** — added `contextvars.copy_context()` + `ctx.run()` when submitting to the thread pool executor. This is the [official MLflow-documented pattern](https://mlflow.org/docs/latest/genai/tracing/faq/) for propagating trace context across thread boundaries.
- **`services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/trigger.py`** — `mlflow.langchain.autolog(run_tracer_inline=True)` to keep the tracer in the main async task instead of offloading to another thread pool.
- **`services/api-service/src/api_service/main.py`** — same `run_tracer_inline=True` change.

### 3. Enabled Claude in MLflow AI Gateway
- **`infra/gateway-config.yaml`** — new file defining Claude Sonnet and Claude Haiku endpoints via the Anthropic provider.
- **`infra/docker-compose.yml`** — added `--gateway-config`, mounted the config file read-only, and passes `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` env var.
- **`.env`** — added placeholder for `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (you'll need to set your actual key).

To use: set your `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` in `.env`, then `docker-compose -f infra/docker-compose.yml up`. The MLflow UI at `http://localhost:5001` will have the gateway endpoints available, and the Claude models will be accessible at `http://localhost:5001/gateway/claude-sonnet/invocations`.

Based on the search results, here's what I found about the mlflow-assistant PyPI package and its relationship to Claude Code subscription:

## mlflow-assistant Package

mlflow-assistant is an MLflow plugin that enables natural language conversations with your MLflow server using LLM providers like OpenAI and Ollama. The package is available on PyPI and the latest version is 0.1.5 (uploaded September 24, 2025).

## MLflow Assistant and Claude Code Subscription

There are actually two related but distinct MLflow Assistant offerings:

1. **MLflow Assistant Built-in Feature**: MLflow Assistant is a powerful AI-powered helper that transforms cutting-edge coding agents like Claude Code into experienced AI engineers by your side.

2. **Subscription Model**: Regarding costs, MLflow Assistant uses your existing coding agent subscription. You pay for the agent (Claude Code, etc.), but MLflow provides the knowledge and integration at no cost. There are no additional fees, premium tiers, or usage limits from MLflow's side.

MLflow Assistant is an in-product chatbot that can help you identify, diagnose, and fix issues. The assistant is backed by Claude Code, and directly passes context from the MLflow UI to Claude.

In summary, the mlflow-assistant package on PyPI is a separate plugin, while the MLflow Assistant feature in MLflow itself leverages your existing Claude Code subscription at no additional cost to provide an integrated AI debugging and analysis experience.

Based on the search results, here's what I found about MLflow Assistant's integration with Claude Code:

## MLflow Assistant and Claude Code Integration

Claude Code is fully supported with more agents coming soon. MLflow Assistant is a Claude Code-powered assistant that is embedded directly in the MLflow UI and helps you with your MLflow-related tasks.

### Key Features

You can run Claude Code directly inside MLflow, with MLflow Assistant making this possible. Claude Code now shares the same context of what you are seeing in the UI, such as which traces, runs, and evaluations you are looking at, and works like another AI engineer sitting next to your desk.

The assistant can fetch MLflow data, read your code, and even add tracing, evaluation, and versioning to your project without human intervention.

### Cost and Privacy

Use your existing coding agent subscription (Claude Code, etc.) and pay nothing extra, as MLflow provides the knowledge and integration at no cost. The assistant runs entirely on your local machine through your coding agent, and your code and data never leave your environment unless you explicitly configure the project path during the setup.

### Tracing Support

After setting up auto tracing, MLflow will automatically capture traces of your Claude Code conversations and log them to the active MLflow experiment.

# MLflow Assistant Setup Instructions

Based on the documentation provided, here are the setup requirements:

## Server Requirements
The page states that MLflow Assistant "is in beta and only available for **local MLflow tracking server**." No specific version requirements or special server flags are mentioned.

## Setup Steps
The documentation outlines a four-step process:

1. **Launch MLflow UI** at `http://localhost:5000` or your custom port
2. **Access the Assistant panel** - "the Assistant panel should open automatically" if using a local server, or navigate via the experiment page sidebar
3. **Complete the configuration wizard** - Select your coding agent backend and authenticate
4. **Begin interaction** - The assistant only functions within experiment pages

## Claude Code Integration
The page indicates that "Claude Code is fully supported," but provides minimal technical details on the connection mechanism. It mentions that during setup, "if you select Claude Code as the backend, you will be asked to log in to Claude Code."

## Configuration Notes
Users can modify settings later "from the Assistant setting page (the gear icon at the top)." The documentation references Claude Code's `.claude/settings.json` file for granular permission control but doesn't specify MLflow-specific configuration files or environment variables.

**Notable limitation:** The documentation explicitly states support for remote servers is not yet available.

# mlflow-assistant Package Overview

**What is it?**
This is a separate, third-party MLflow plugin—not built-in to MLflow. According to the PyPI page, it "enables natural language conversations with your MLflow server using LLM providers like OpenAI and Ollama."

**Current Version:**
0.1.5 (released September 24, 2025)

**Installation:**
```
pip install mlflow-assistant
```

**Key Features:**
- Interact with MLflow servers through natural language queries
- Integrates with large language models (LLMs)
- Straightforward setup with MLflow

**Requirements:**
Python 3.12.4 or higher (but documentation mentions Python ≥3.9 support); MLflow ≥2.21.0, <3.0.0

**Important Note:**
This is a community-developed tool maintained by Hugo Carvalho under the Apache 2.0 license, not an official Anthropic or MLflow product. It facilitates conversational interaction with MLflow servers rather than being a built-in component of MLflow itself.

# Claude Code with MLflow Assistant Setup

Based on the blog post, here's what's needed to enable MLflow Assistant:

## Basic Configuration

The article states you should "Get started by configuring the Assistant in the MLflow UI and follow the [docs](https://mlflow.org/docs/latest/genai/getting-started/try-assistant.html) to customize it to your needs."

## Key Points

**No Additional Setup Required:**
The guide emphasizes that "Since it uses your existing Claude Code subscription, **no extra API keys and no additional cost**. Everything runs locally with full transparency."

**Context Integration:**
MLflow Assistant provides seamless integration by sharing the same context as the MLflow UI, eliminating the need to copy-paste trace IDs, error messages, or evaluation data between terminal and dashboard.

## Related Infrastructure

While the MLflow Assistant itself doesn't require MCP configuration, the blog mentions that Claude Code can optionally connect to MLflow through:
- **MCP Server** (configured in `.claude/settings.json`)
- **MLflow CLI commands** for ad-hoc workflows

The specific detailed setup instructions are referenced in the official MLflow documentation rather than provided in this blog post.

Gateway config reverted. Here's the deal with MLflow Assistant:

## MLflow Assistant (built into MLflow 3.9.0)

It's already available in your MLflow UI — no server-side config needed. It uses your **local Claude Code CLI** and your existing subscription.

**To use it:**
1. Open MLflow UI at `http://localhost:5001`
2. Navigate to any experiment page
3. The Assistant panel should appear (or look for a chat/assistant icon)
4. Select **Claude Code** as the backend
5. Log in with your Claude subscription when prompted

**Important caveat:** The docs say it only works with a **local MLflow tracking server** — not remote. Since you're running MLflow on localhost:5001, this should work. It connects to your locally installed Claude Code CLI via MCP, so make sure Claude Code is installed on your machine.

**What it can do:** It shares the context of what you're viewing in the MLflow UI (traces, runs, evaluations) and can help diagnose issues, analyze traces, and suggest fixes — all using your existing Claude subscription at no extra cost.

Sources:
- [MLflow Assistant docs](https://mlflow.org/docs/latest/genai/getting-started/try-assistant/)
- [5 Tips to Get More Out of Your Claude Code with MLflow](https://mlflow.org/blog/mlflow-claude-code)
- [Tracing Claude Code | MLflow](https://mlflow.org/docs/latest/genai/tracing/integrations/listing/claude_code/)

> DEVELOPER

The issue is we are using mlflow in a docker

> AGENT

Right — the MLflow Assistant beta explicitly requires a **local MLflow server process**, not one running inside Docker. The Assistant connects the browser UI to your local Claude Code CLI, and that bridge breaks when the server is containerized.

The simplest fix: **run MLflow outside Docker** on your host machine. It's a single lightweight process.

MLflow 3.9.0 is available via `uv run`. Here's the new setup:

## What changed

- **Removed** the `mlflow` service from `docker-compose.yml`
- **Updated** the API container's `MLFLOW_TRACKING_URI` to `http://host.docker.internal:5001` so Docker containers can reach the host-local MLflow
- **Removed** the `mlflow_data` volume

## How to run MLflow now

Start MLflow on your host **before** docker-compose:

```bash
uv run mlflow server --port 5001
```

Then open `http://localhost:5001`, navigate to any experiment page, and the Assistant panel will be available. Select Claude Code as the backend and log in with your subscription.

For docker-compose deployments:
```bash
uv run mlflow server --port 5001 &
docker-compose -f infra/docker-compose.yml up --build
```

Sources:
- [MLflow Assistant docs](https://mlflow.org/docs/latest/genai/getting-started/try-assistant/)
- [5 Tips to Get More Out of Your Claude Code with MLflow](https://mlflow.org/blog/mlflow-claude-code)

Perfect! Now I have a comprehensive understanding. Let me create a detailed summary report:

## Docker Compose and Infrastructure Setup - Complete Analysis

Based on my thorough exploration of the codebase, here's a comprehensive breakdown of the Docker Compose and infrastructure setup:

### 1. **Docker Compose Structure** (`/infra/docker-compose.yml`)

The stack includes 4 services:

| Service | Type | Role |
|---------|------|------|
| **api** | Built | FastAPI service (Python) |
| **ui** | Built | HITL UI (Node.js) - Dockerfile TBD |
| **db** | Image | PostgreSQL 16-alpine |
| **pubsub-emulator** | Image | Google Cloud Pub/Sub emulator |

**Key Insight:** MLflow is **NOT** a Docker service. It runs on the host machine instead.

### 2. **MLflow Integration - Unique Architecture**

The design explicitly keeps MLflow **outside Docker** for a specific reason:

```yaml
# From docker-compose.yml header:
# MLflow runs on the host (not Docker) to enable MLflow Assistant + Claude Code.
# Start MLflow first: uv run mlflow server --port 5001
# Then: docker-compose -f infra/docker-compose.yml up --build
```

**Docker-to-Host Communication:**
- API container reaches MLflow via: `http://host.docker.internal:${MLFLOW_PORT:-5001}`
- This uses Docker's special DNS name `host.docker.internal` (available on Docker Desktop)
- Default port: `5001`

**Environment Variables in API Service:**
```yaml
- MLFLOW_TRACKING_URI=http://host.docker.internal:${MLFLOW_PORT:-5001}
- MLFLOW_TRACE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS=900
```

### 3. **MLflow Configuration in Code**

**In `/libs/shared/src/shared/resilience.py`:**
- Circuit breaker for MLflow operations
- Uses `MLFLOW_TRACKING_URI` environment variable
- Has `MLflowCircuitBreakerListener` that logs state changes to MLflow
- Graceful no-op if MLflow unavailable

**In `/services/api-service/src/api_service/main.py`:**
```python
tracking_uri = os.getenv("MLFLOW_TRACKING_URI")
if tracking_uri:
    mlflow.set_tracking_uri(tracking_uri)
    mlflow.set_experiment("protocol-processing")
    mlflow.langchain.autolog(run_tracer_inline=True)  # LangChain integration
```

**In `/services/api-service/src/api_service/middleware.py`:**
- Custom ASGI middleware `MLflowRequestMiddleware` (NOT BaseHTTPMiddleware)
- Traces all HTTP requests except `/health`, `/ready`, `/`
- Creates MLflow spans for each request with method, path, query, status_code, latency_ms
- Pure ASGI to preserve contextvars (required for proper MLflow tracing)

### 4. **Docker Compose Configuration Details**

**Build Context:**
- API: Dockerfile at `services/api-service/Dockerfile`
- UI: Dockerfile at `apps/hitl-ui/Dockerfile` (referenced but doesn't exist yet)

**API Service Configuration:**
- Port: `${API_PORT:-8000}`
- Loads env from `../.env`
- Mounts GCP credentials: `~/.config/gcloud/application_default_credentials.json` (read-only)
- Mounts uploads directory: `../data/uploads`
- Health check: `curl http://localhost:8000/health` (30s interval)
- Depends on: `db` with condition `service_healthy`

**Database:**
- PostgreSQL 16-alpine
- Port: `${POSTGRES_PORT:-5432}`
- Named volume: `postgres_data`
- Health check using `pg_isready`

**Pub/Sub Emulator:**
- Image: `3apag/pubsub-emulator:latest`
- Port: `${PUBSUB_EMULATOR_PORT:-8432}`
- Named volume: `pubsub_data`
- Health check: `nc -z localhost 8432`

### 5. **Docker Compose Override Files**

**Status:** NONE FOUND
- No `docker-compose.override.yml`
- No `docker-compose.dev.yml`
- No `docker-compose.prod.yml`
- Single file approach: only `infra/docker-compose.yml`

### 6. **Docker Compose Profiles**

**Status:** NOT USED
- No profiles are defined in the docker-compose.yml
- All services start by default

### 7. **Dockerfile Analysis**

**`/services/api-service/Dockerfile`:**
- Base: `python:3.12-slim`
- Uses `uv` for fast dependency management
- Installs: `curl` (health checks), `gcc`, `build-essential` (C extensions)
- Multi-stage: not fully optimized (can be improved)
- Runtime command: Runs Alembic migrations then starts uvicorn
- Exposes: Port 8000

```dockerfile
CMD ["sh", "-c", "cd services/api-service && uv run --no-sync alembic upgrade head && uv run --no-sync uvicorn api_service.main:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8000"]
```

**UI Dockerfile:** Referenced but doesn't exist yet (needs creation)

### 8. **Startup Scripts and Orchestration**

**Makefile Commands (`/Makefile`):**

| Command | Purpose |
|---------|---------|
| `make run-dev` | Start infra (db + MLflow) + API + UI (comprehensive dev setup) |
| `make run-infra` | Start only DB and MLflow services |
| `make run-api` | Start API service alone (port 8000) |
| `make run-ui` | Start UI dev server (port 3000) |
| `make mlflow-clear` | Delete all MLflow runs/traces |

**Key Flow in `make run-dev`:**
1. Checks Docker is running
2. Activates gcloud profile from `.env` if set
3. Starts DB and MLflow with `docker compose ... up -d db mlflow`
4. Waits for Postgres to be ready
5. Finds available ports (auto-increments if occupied)
6. Starts API with uvicorn in reload mode
7. Starts UI with `npm run dev`

**Note:** The Makefile references `docker compose ... up -d db mlflow` but MLflow is NOT a Docker service. This appears to be a bug/mismatch in the Makefile (it tries to start a non-existent mlflow service).

### 9. **Environment Variables**

**Primary `.env` file** (`/root-level .env`):
```
UMLS_API_KEY=...
USE_AI_GROUNDING=true
GCP_PROJECT_ID=...
MLFLOW_TRACKING_URI=http://localhost:5001
DATABASE_URL=postgresql://postgres:postgres@localhost:5432/app
LOG_LEVEL=INFO
USE_LOCAL_STORAGE=1
```

**Infra-specific `.env.example`** (`/infra/.env.example`):
```
POSTGRES_USER=postgres
POSTGRES_PASSWORD=postgres
POSTGRES_DB=clinical_trials
API_PORT=8000
UI_PORT=3000
VITE_API_BASE_URL=http://localhost:8000
MLFLOW_PORT=5001 (implicit from docker-compose)
```

### 10. **Infra README** (`/infra/README.md`)

Minimal documentation stating:
- Docker Compose for API, UI, Postgres, MLflow, Pub/Sub
- Start from repo root: `docker-compose -f infra/docker-compose.yml up --build`
- Optional env vars: `API_PORT`, `UI_PORT`, `POSTGRES_*`, `VITE_API_BASE_URL`
- Mentions Terraform/K8s as future options

### 11. **Key Architectural Patterns**

**MLflow in CI/CD:**
- MLflow Circuit Breaker - fails gracefully
- Optional integration (checks `MLFLOW_TRACKING_URI` is set)
- LangChain autolog enabled for agent traces
- Request middleware captures HTTP span data

**Service Dependencies:**
- UI depends on API (health check required)
- API depends on DB (health check required)
- Both use `service_healthy` condition (not just `service_started`)

**Volume Mounts:**
- Uploads directory shared between host and API container
- GCP credentials mounted read-only
- Postgres data persisted in named volume

### Summary

The infrastructure is **well-designed but has a configuration mismatch:**
- MLflow **should** run on the host (correct decision for Claude Code integration)
- But the Makefile tries to start it as a Docker service (which doesn't exist)
- The docker-compose.yml is missing the `mlflow` service definition referenced in the Makefile

**Strengths:**
- Health checks on all services
- Service dependencies properly declared
- Graceful degradation if MLflow unavailable
- GCP credentials handling via volume mount

**Areas for Improvement:**
1. Create missing `apps/hitl-ui/Dockerfile`
2. Fix Makefile: remove references to `docker compose ... mlflow` OR add MLflow to docker-compose.yml
3. Consider docker-compose override files for dev/prod separation
4. Update infra/README.md with updated instructions

    60→	uv run python scripts/verify_gemini_access.py
    61→
    62→create-component:
    63→	@read -p "Enter component name: " COMPONENT_NAME; \
    64→	./scripts/create-service.sh --lang py "$$COMPONENT_NAME"
    65→
    66→create-service:
    67→	@read -p "Enter service name: " NAME; read -p "Language (py|ts) [py]: " LANG; LANG=$${LANG:-py}; \
    68→	./scripts/create-service.sh --lang "$$LANG" "$$NAME"
    69→
    70→docs-components-gen:
    71→	@echo "Generating components overview page..."
    72→	uv run python scripts/generate_components_overview.py
    73→
    74→docs-nav-update:
    75→	@echo "Updating root navigation..."
    76→	uv run python scripts/update_root_navigation.py
    77→
    78→docs-build: docs-nav-update docs-components-gen docs-openapi
    79→	@echo "Building documentation site..."
    80→	uv run python scripts/build_docs.py build -f mkdocs.yml
    81→
    82→docs-serve:
    83→	@echo "Serving built documentation site..."
    84→	@if [ ! -d "site" ]; then \
    85→		echo "No built site found. Run 'make docs-build' first."; \
    86→		exit 1; \
    87→	fi
    88→	@echo "Serving from: $(PWD)/site"
    89→	@echo "Available at: http://localhost:8000"
    90→	@echo "Press Ctrl+C to stop"
    91→	@cd site && uv run python -m http.server 8000
    92→
    93→docs-openapi:
    94→	@echo "Exporting OpenAPI spec..."
    95→	uv run --project services/api-service python services/api-service/scripts/export_openapi.py
    96→
    97→kill-processes:
    98→	@echo "Killing running processes..."
    99→	@./scripts/kill-running-processes.sh
   100→
   101→db-revision:
   102→	@echo "Creating new Alembic revision..."
   103→	uv run alembic revision --autogenerate -m "$(msg)"
   104→
   105→db-migrate:
   106→	@echo "Applying database migrations..."
   107→	uv run alembic upgrade head
   108→
   109→db-clear-protocols:
   110→	@echo "Clearing all protocols, criteria, and related data from the database..."
   111→	@set -a && . ./.env && set +a && uv run --project services/api-service python services/api-service/scripts/clear_protocols_and_criteria.py
   112→
   113→mlflow-clear:
   114→	@echo "Deleting all MLflow runs/traces..."
   115→	@set -a && . ./.env && set +a && uv run --group dev python scripts/clear_mlflow_traces.py
   116→
   117→clean:
   118→	@echo "Cleaning build artifacts..."
   119→	rm -rf site/
   120→	rm -rf .cache/
   121→	rm -rf docs/.uv_cache/
   122→
   123→# Code Quality Commands
   124→check:
   125→	@echo "Running all checks..."
   126→	@./scripts/check-all.sh
   127→
   128→check-fix:
   129→	@echo "Running all checks with auto-fix..."
   130→	@./scripts/check-all.sh --fix
   131→
   132→check-with-docs:
   133→	@echo "Running all checks including documentation..."
   134→	@./scripts/check-all.sh --with-docs
   135→
   136→lint:
   137→	@echo "Running linters..."
   138→	uv run ruff check .
   139→	@if [ -d "apps/hitl-ui/node_modules" ]; then \
   140→		cd apps/hitl-ui && npm run lint; \
   141→	fi
   142→
   143→lint-fix:
   144→	@echo "Running linters with auto-fix..."
   145→	uv run ruff check . --fix
   146→	uv run ruff format .
   147→	@if [ -d "apps/hitl-ui/node_modules" ]; then \
   148→		cd apps/hitl-ui && npm run lint:fix; \
   149→	fi
   150→
   151→typecheck:
   152→	@echo "Running type checkers..."
   153→	uv run mypy libs/shared/src services/api-service/src libs/inference/src services/extraction-service/src services/grounding-service/src libs/data-pipeline/src libs/evaluation/src libs/events-py/src libs/model-training/src
   154→	@if [ -d "apps/hitl-ui/node_modules" ]; then \
   155→		cd apps/hitl-ui && npx tsc --noEmit; \
   156→	fi
   157→
   158→test:
   159→	@echo "Running tests..."
   160→	uv run pytest services/api-service/tests libs/events-py/tests -q
   161→	@if [ -d "apps/hitl-ui/node_modules" ]; then \
   162→		cd apps/hitl-ui && npm test -- --run; \
   163→	fi
   164→
   165→# Quality Evaluation
   166→quality-eval:  ## Run quality evaluation on sample PDFs (requires Docker Compose stack)
   167→	uv run python scripts/quality_eval.py
   168→
   169→quality-eval-fresh:  ## Run quality evaluation with fresh pipeline runs
   170→	uv run python scripts/quality_eval.py --fresh
   171→
   172→help:
   173→	@echo "ElixirTrials  - Makefile Commands"
   174→	@echo ""
   175→	@echo "Development:"
   176→	@echo "  make run-dev   - Start infra + API + UI (all-in-one)"
   177→	@echo "  make run-infra - Start DB, MLflow only"
   178→	@echo "  make run-api   - Start API service (port 8000)"
   179→	@echo "  make run-ui    - Start UI dev server (port 3000)"
   180→	@echo ""
   181→	@echo "Code Quality:"

   183→	@echo "  make check-with-docs - Run all checks including doc build"
   184→	@echo "  make check-fix       - Run all checks with auto-fix"
   185→	@echo "  make lint       - Run ruff and Biome (hitl-ui)"
   186→	@echo "  make lint-fix   - Run linters with auto-fix"
   187→	@echo "  make typecheck  - Run mypy and tsc"
   188→	@echo "  make test       - Run pytest and vitest"
   189→	@echo ""
   190→	@echo "Documentation:"

   192→	@echo "  make docs-serve - Serve documentation locally"
   193→	@echo ""

   197→	@echo "  make db-clear-protocols  - Remove all protocols, criteria, and related data"
